The optimal value for the
4
-
\mathrm{XOR}
game satisfies the proportionality,

\displaystyle\omega\big({4\mathrm{XOR}}\big)\propto\underset{A,B,C,D,\ket{\psi_{4\mathrm{XOR}}}}{\mathrm{sup}}\bra{\psi_{4\mathrm{XOR}}}\bigg(C_{\sigma(i)\sigma(j)\sigma(k)}\otimes A_{i}\otimes B_{ij}\otimes D_{ijkl}+C_{ijk}\otimes A_{\sigma(i)}

\displaystyle\otimes B_{ij}\otimes D_{ijkl}+C_{ijk}\otimes A_{i}\otimes B_{\sigma(i)\sigma(j)}\otimes D_{ijkl}+C_{ijk}\otimes A_{i}\otimes B_{ij}\otimes D_{\sigma(i)\sigma(j)\sigma(k)\sigma(l)}

\displaystyle+C_{\sigma(i)\sigma(j)\sigma(k)}\otimes A_{\sigma(i)}\otimes B_{ij}\otimes D_{ijkl}+C_{\sigma(i)\sigma(j)\sigma(k)}\otimes A_{\sigma(i)}\otimes B_{\sigma(i)\sigma(j)}\otimes D_{\sigma(i)\sigma(j)\sigma(k)\sigma(l)}

\displaystyle+C_{ijk}\otimes A_{\sigma(i)}\otimes B_{\sigma(i)\sigma(j)}\otimes D_{\sigma(i)\sigma(j)\sigma(k)\sigma(l)}+C_{ijk}\otimes A_{\sigma(i)}\otimes B_{ij}\otimes D_{ijkl}+C_{ijk}\otimes A_{\sigma(i)}

\displaystyle\otimes B_{\sigma(i)\sigma(j)}\otimes D_{ijkl}+C_{ijk}\otimes A_{i}\otimes B_{\sigma(i)\sigma(j)}\otimes D_{ijkl}+C_{ijk}\otimes A_{i}

\displaystyle\otimes B_{\sigma(i)\sigma(j)}\otimes D_{\sigma(i)\sigma(j)\sigma(k)\sigma(l)}\bigg)\ket{\psi_{4\mathrm{XOR}}}\text{. }

The set of three equivalent conditions above for the
3
-
\mathrm{XOR}
game above extend to
\mathrm{XOR}
games with an even, or odd, number of players. Given the optimal strategy for a
2

\mathrm{XOR}
game,
\ket{\psi_{2\mathrm{XOR}}}
, [37],
2.1
, the relations,

\displaystyle\bigg(A_{i}\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg)\ket{\psi_{2\mathrm{XOR}}}=\bigg(\textbf{I}\otimes\frac{B_{ij}+B_{ji}}{\sqrt{2}}\bigg)\ket{\psi_{2\mathrm{XOR}}}\text{, }

\displaystyle\bigg(A_{j}\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg)\ket{\psi_{2\mathrm{XOR}}}=\bigg(\textbf{I}\otimes\frac{B_{ij}-B_{ji}}{\sqrt{2}}\bigg)\ket{\psi_{2\mathrm{XOR}}}\text{, }

are generalized in the third item below. To list the set of equivalent conditions for the
5

\mathrm{XOR}
game below, introduce,

\displaystyle\underline{\mathscr{P}_{5\mathrm{XOR}}}\equiv\underline{\mathscr{P}_{5\mathrm{XOR},1}}\cup\underline{\mathscr{P}_{5\mathrm{XOR},1,2}}\cup\underline{\mathscr{P}_{5\mathrm{XOR},1,2,3}}\cup\cdots\cup\underline{\mathscr{P}_{5\mathrm{XOR},1,2,3,4,5}}\text{, }

corresponding to the permutations,

\displaystyle\underline{\mathscr{P}_{5\mathrm{XOR},1}}\equiv\underset{\text{Permutations }\sigma\in S_{5}}{\sum}\bigg(E_{ijkl}\otimes C_{ijk}\otimes A_{\sigma(i)}\otimes B_{ij}\otimes D_{ijkl}\bigg)\text{, }

\displaystyle\underline{\mathscr{P}_{5\mathrm{XOR},1,2}}\equiv\underset{\text{Permutations }\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in S_{5}}{\sum}\bigg(E_{ijkl}\otimes C_{ijk}\otimes A_{\sigma(i)}\otimes B_{\sigma^{\prime}(ij)}\otimes D_{ijkl}\bigg)\text{, }

\displaystyle\underline{\mathscr{P}_{5\mathrm{XOR},1,2,3}}\equiv\underset{\text{Permutations }\sigma,\sigma^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime\prime}\in S_{5}}{\sum}\bigg(E_{ijkl}\otimes C_{\sigma^{\prime\prime}(ijk)}\otimes A_{\sigma(i)}\otimes B_{\sigma^{\prime}(ij)}

\displaystyle\otimes D_{ijkl}\bigg)\text{, }

\displaystyle\underline{\mathscr{P}_{5\mathrm{XOR},1,2,3,4,5}}\equiv\underset{\text{Permutations }\sigma,\sigma^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime\prime},\sigma^{\prime\prime\prime},\sigma^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}\in S_{5}}{\sum}\bigg(E_{\sigma^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}(ijkl)}\otimes C_{\sigma^{\prime\prime}(ijk)}\otimes A_{\sigma(i)}\otimes B_{\sigma^{\prime}(ij)}

\displaystyle\otimes D_{\sigma^{\prime\prime\prime}(ijkl)}\bigg)\text{. }

For the
5
-
\mathrm{XOR}
game, the set of equivalent conditions is provided in the first section of the Appendix.

Beyond the two-player setting, mixed optimal solution states that groups of three players can adopt are of the form,

\displaystyle\bra{\psi_{>3\mathrm{XOR}}}G_{>3\mathrm{XOR}}\ket{\psi_{>3\mathrm{XOR}}}\equiv\bra{\psi_{>3\mathrm{XOR}}}\bigg(G_{3\mathrm{XOR}}\bra{ijkl}+G_{3\mathrm{XOR}}\bra{jikl}+G_{3\mathrm{XOR}}\bra{ikjl}

\displaystyle+G_{3\mathrm{XOR}}\bra{ijlk}\bigg)\ket{\psi_{>3\mathrm{XOR}}}\text{. }

The intermediate terms to the braket state of the optimal quantum state corresponding to the strategies of each player arise from entries of the 3-player
\mathrm{XOR}
game tensor, which satisfies,

\displaystyle G_{3\mathrm{XOR}}\propto\underset{i_{1}\in\mathcal{Q}_{1},i_{2}\in\mathcal{Q}_{2},i_{3}\in\mathcal{Q}_{3}}{\sum}\bigg[\ket{i_{1}}\bigg(\bra{i_{1}i_{2}}\bra{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}\bigg)+\ket{i_{2}}\bigg(\bra{i_{2}i_{1}}\bra{i_{2}i_{1}i_{3}}\bigg)+\ket{i_{3}}\bigg(\bra{i_{3}i_{2}}\bra{i_{3}i_{2}i_{1}}\bigg)

\displaystyle+\text{Higher order permutations}\bigg]

\displaystyle\equiv\underset{\text{acting on each }\ket{\psi_{i}}}{\underset{\text{permutations }\sigma_{i}}{\prod}}\bigg[\underset{\ket{\psi}\in\textbf{C}^{nm\times(n+m)}}{\underset{1\leq i_{1}<i_{2}<i_{3}\leq 3}{\sum}}\big\{1\leq i^{\prime}\leq n+m:\sigma_{i}\psi\big\}\bigg]

\displaystyle\equiv{\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}}\bigg[\underset{\text{acting on each }\ket{\psi}_{i}}{\underset{\text{permutations }\sigma_{i}}{\prod}}\bigg[\underset{\ket{\psi}\in\textbf{C}^{nm\times(n+m)}}{\underset{1\leq i_{1}<i_{2}<i_{3}\leq 3}{\sum}}\sigma\ket{\psi}_{i}\bigg]\bigg]\text{. }

\epsilon
-approximality of the
>3

\mathrm{XOR}
game bias takes the form,

\displaystyle\big(1-\epsilon_{>3\mathrm{XOR}}\big)\beta\big(G_{>3\mathrm{XOR}}\big)\leq\underset{i_{1}\in\mathcal{Q}_{1},i_{2}\in\mathcal{Q}_{2},i_{3}\in\mathcal{Q}_{3}}{\sum}\bra{\psi_{>3\mathrm{XOR}}}\bigg(\underset{\#\text{ players}}{\bigotimes}\text{Tensors of player}

\displaystyle\text{ observables}\big(i_{1},i_{2},i_{3}\big)\bigg)\ket{\psi_{>3\mathrm{XOR}}}\leq\beta\big(G_{>3\mathrm{XOR}}\big)\text{, }

given the existence of a constant
\epsilon
taken to be sufficiently small. For the following quantities, denote the set of questions,
\mathcal{Q}_{1}
, as the set of questions distributed to the first player, and so on. The Schmidt basis takes the form,

\displaystyle\underset{i_{1}\in\mathcal{Q}_{1},i_{2}\in\mathcal{Q}_{2},i_{3}\in\mathcal{Q}_{3}}{\sum}\sqrt{\lambda_{i}}\bigg(\underset{\#\text{ players}}{\bigotimes}\text{Tensors of player observables}\big(i_{1},i_{2},i_{3}\big)\bigg)\text{. }

In [37], objects involved with defining the two-dimensional
\mathrm{CHSH}
Schmidt basis take the form,

\displaystyle\overset{s2^{\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\rfloor}}{\underset{i=1}{\sum}}\sqrt{\lambda_{i}}\big(\ket{u_{i}}\otimes\ket{v_{i}}\big)\text{, }

into a tensor product over the quantum states which respectively corresponding to each
u_{i}
and
v_{i}
are equal in blocks
\lambda
between each term of the summation over
i
above, as (
Theorem

5
, [37]),

\displaystyle\lambda_{i}=\cdots=\lambda_{i+1}\text{, }\forall i\text{. }

Moreover, with respect to some basis
\{\ket{u_{i}}\}_{i\in\mathcal{I}}
of
\textbf{C}^{d_{A}}
, the observable
A_{i}
decomposes as,

\displaystyle A_{i}=\mathrm{diag}\big(A^{(1)}_{i},\cdots,A^{(s)}_{i},C_{i}\big)\text{, }

where each block diagonal component
A^{(s)}_{i}
, of size
2^{\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\rfloor}\times 2^{\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\rfloor}
, is such that each
A_{i}
acts on
\mathrm{span}\big(\ket{u_{(j-1)2^{\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\rfloor}+1}},\cdots

,\ket{u_{j2^{\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\rfloor}}}\big)
for
1\leq i\leq n
, with,

\displaystyle C_{i}\equiv\big\{\pm 1\big\}\text{, }

\forall\big(s-1\big)2^{\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\rfloor}\leq i\leq s2^{\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\rfloor}
. For a basis
\{\ket{v_{i}}\}_{i\in\mathcal{I}}
of
\textbf{C}^{d_{B}}
, the observable
B_{jk}
decomposes as,

\displaystyle B_{jk}=\mathrm{diag}\big(B^{(1)}_{jk},\cdots,B^{(s)}_{jk},D_{jk}\big)\text{, }

where each block diagonal component
B^{(s)}_{jk}
, also of size
2^{\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\rfloor}\times 2^{\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\rfloor}
, is such that each
B_{jk}
acts on
\mathrm{span}\big(\ket{v_{(j-1)2^{\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\rfloor+1}}},\cdots,\ket{v_{j2^{\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\rfloor}}}\big)
for
1\leq j\neq k\leq n
, with,

\displaystyle D_{jk}\equiv\big\{\pm 1\big\}\text{. }

The construction above can be adapted for obtaining tensors whose dimensionality is spanned by the set of all possible responses from previous players who have participated in the game.

In the
N
-player setting, straightforwardly the quantities involving with expressing inequalities of the form above, before, and after, taking the supremum over all admissible strategies
\mathcal{S}
, take the form,

\displaystyle\big(1-\epsilon_{N\mathrm{XOR}}\big)\beta\big(G_{N\mathrm{XOR}}\big)\leq\underset{i_{1}\in\mathcal{Q}_{1},i_{2}\in\mathcal{Q}_{2},\cdots,i_{n}\in\mathcal{Q}_{n}}{\sum}\bra{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}}}\bigg(\underset{\#\text{ players}}{\bigotimes}\text{Tensors of }

\displaystyle\text{player observables}\big(i_{1},i_{2},\cdots,i_{n}\big)\bigg)\ket{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}}}\leq\beta\big(G_{N\mathrm{XOR}}\big)\text{.}

The
N
-player Schmidt basis takes the form,

The generalization of the Schmidt basis provided above, for any number of players, implies,

for,

Proof of Theorem
1^{*}
. To argue that the desired upper bound holds from direct computation, recall the following result, as a generalization of the
\epsilon
-approximality result of [37]:

Theorem

1
(
approximately optimal quantum strategies for the nonlocal XOR game
,
Theorem

4
, [44]). For
\pm
observables
A_{i}
and
B_{jk}
, given a bipartite state
\psi
, TFAE:

\bullet

First characterization of approximate optimality
:
An
\epsilon
-approximate
\mathrm{CHSH}\big(n\big)
satisfies
\mathrm{(0)}
.

\bullet

Second characterization of approximate optimality
:
For an
\epsilon
-approximate quantum strategy,

\displaystyle\underset{1\leq i<j\leq n}{\sum}\bigg[\text{ }\bigg|\bigg|\bigg[\big(\frac{A_{i}+A_{j}}{\sqrt{2}}\big)\otimes I\bigg]\ket{\psi}-\big[I\otimes B_{ij}\big]\ket{\psi}\bigg|\bigg|^{2}+\bigg|\bigg|\bigg[\big(\frac{A_{i}-A_{j}}{\sqrt{2}}\big)\otimes I\bigg]\ket{\psi}

\displaystyle-\big[I\otimes B_{ji}\big]\ket{\psi}\bigg|\bigg|^{2}\text{ }\bigg]\leq 2n\big(n-1\big)\epsilon\text{. }

\bullet

Reversing the order of the tensor product for observables
:
Related to the inequality for
\epsilon
-approximate strategies above, another inequality,

\displaystyle\underset{1\leq i<j\leq n}{\sum}\bigg[\text{ }\bigg|\bigg|\big[A_{i}\otimes I\big]\ket{\psi}-\bigg[I\otimes\big(\frac{B_{ij}+B_{ji}}{\sqrt{2}}\big)\bigg]\ket{\psi}\bigg|\bigg|^{2}+\bigg|\bigg|\big[A_{j}\otimes I\big]\ket{\psi}

\displaystyle-\bigg[I\otimes\big(\frac{B_{ij}-B_{ji}}{\sqrt{2}}\big)\bigg]\ket{\psi}\big|\big|^{2}\text{ }\bigg]\leq 2n\big(n-1\big)\epsilon\text{, }

also holds.

\bullet

Characterization of exact optimality
: For
\epsilon\equiv 0
,

\displaystyle\underset{1\leq i<j\leq n}{\sum}\bigg[\text{ }\bigg|\bigg|\bigg[\big(\frac{A_{i}+A_{j}}{\sqrt{2}}\big)\otimes I\bigg]\ket{\psi}-\big[I\otimes B_{ij}\big]\ket{\psi}\bigg|\bigg|^{2}\bigg]=-\underset{1\leq i<j\leq n}{\sum}\bigg[\text{ }\bigg|\bigg|\bigg[\big(\frac{A_{i}-A_{j}}{\sqrt{2}}\big)\otimes I\bigg]\ket{\psi}

\displaystyle-\big[I\otimes B_{ji}\big]\ket{\psi}\bigg|\bigg|^{2}\bigg]\text{, }

corresponding to the first inequality, and,

\displaystyle\underset{1\leq i<j\leq n}{\sum}\bigg[\text{ }\bigg|\bigg|\big[A_{i}\otimes I\big]\ket{\psi}-\bigg[I\otimes\big(\frac{B_{ij}+B_{ji}}{\sqrt{2}}\big)\bigg]\ket{\psi}\bigg|\bigg|^{2}\bigg]=-\underset{1\leq i<j\leq n}{\sum}\bigg[\bigg|\bigg|\big[A_{j}\otimes I\big]\ket{\psi}

\displaystyle-\bigg[I\otimes\big(\frac{B_{ij}-B_{ji}}{\sqrt{2}}\big)\bigg]\ket{\psi}\bigg|\bigg|^{2}\bigg]\text{, }

corresponding to the second inequality.

With the result above, to further generalize the set of equivalent conditions as provided in previous discussions for the
3\mathrm{XOR}
game, and beyond, given the existence of suitable

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\bigg(C_{N\mathrm{XOR}}\omega_{N\mathrm{XOR}}\bigg)n\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq N-1}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)}\underset{i_{N}\in\mathcal{Q}_{N}}{\underset{\vdots}{\underset{i_{1}\in\mathcal{Q}_{1}}{\sum}}}\bigg(\big(u^{\prime}_{i_{1}i_{2}\cdots i_{N}}-v^{\prime}_{i_{1}i_{2}\cdots i_{N}}\big)\big(u^{\prime}_{i_{1}i_{2}\cdots i_{N}}-v^{\prime}_{i_{1}i_{2}\cdots i_{N}}\big)^{\textbf{T}}

\displaystyle\times\big(u^{\prime}_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}\cdots i_{N}i_{N-1}}-v^{\prime}_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}\cdots i_{N}i_{N-1}}\big)^{\textbf{T}}\bigg)\text{,}

given the superposition of states,

\displaystyle u^{\prime}_{i_{1}i_{2}\cdots i_{N}}\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq N}{\sum}\ket{\text{Player }j\text{ state}}\bigg)\text{, }v^{\prime}_{i_{1}i_{2}\cdots i_{N}}\equiv\ket{i_{1}i_{2}\cdots i_{N}}

\displaystyle u^{\prime}_{i_{2}i_{1}i_{3}\cdots i_{N}}\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\bigg(\ket{\text{Player 1 state}}-\ket{\text{Player 2 state}}

\displaystyle+\underset{3\leq j\leq N}{\sum}\ket{\text{Player }j\text{ state}}\bigg)\text{, }v^{\prime}_{i_{2}i_{1}i_{3}\cdots i_{N}}\equiv\ket{i_{2}i_{1}i_{3}\cdots i_{N}}

\displaystyle u^{\prime}_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}\cdots i_{N}i_{N-1}}\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq N-1}{\sum}\ket{\text{Player }j\text{ state}}

\displaystyle-\ket{\text{Player }N\text{ state}}\bigg)\text{, }v^{\prime}_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}\cdots i_{N}i_{N-1}}\equiv\ket{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}\cdots i_{N}i_{N-1}}\text{, }

from which we conclude the argument.

An implication of the above result which demonstrates that the desired upper bound, dependent upon both a combinatorial factor and the total number of players in the game, also implies the following result.

Lemma

T-1
(
positive semidefiniteness
). Under the assumptions of the previous result, the operator,

\displaystyle\underset{1\leq i\leq n^{N}}{\sum}y_{N\mathrm{XOR},i}E_{N\mathrm{XOR},ii}-G_{N\mathrm{XOR},\mathrm{Sym}}\text{, }

is positive semidefinite.

Proof of Lemma T-1
. The result follows from the fact that, for N-XOR games, the computation involving,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\bigg(C_{N\mathrm{XOR}}\omega_{N\mathrm{XOR}}\bigg)n\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq N-1}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)}\underset{i_{N}\in\mathcal{Q}_{N}}{\underset{\vdots}{\underset{i_{1}\in\mathcal{Q}_{1}}{\sum}}}\bigg(\big(u^{\prime}_{i_{1}i_{2}\cdots i_{N}}-v^{\prime}_{i_{1}i_{2}\cdots i_{N}}\big)\big(u^{\prime}_{i_{1}i_{2}\cdots i_{N}}-v^{\prime}_{i_{1}i_{2}\cdots i_{N}}\big)^{\textbf{T}}

\displaystyle+\big(u^{\prime}_{i_{2}i_{1}i_{3}\cdots i_{N}}-v^{\prime}_{i_{2}i_{1}i_{3}\cdots i_{N}}\big)\big(u^{\prime}_{i_{2}i_{1}i_{3}\cdots i_{N}}-v^{\prime}_{i_{2}i_{1}i_{3}\cdots i_{N}}\big)^{\textbf{T}}+\cdots+\big(u^{\prime}_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}\cdots i_{N}i_{N-1}}

\displaystyle-v^{\prime}_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}\cdots i_{N}i_{N-1}}\big)\big(u^{\prime}_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}\cdots i_{N}i_{N-1}}-v^{\prime}_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}\cdots i_{N}i_{N-1}}\big)^{\textbf{T}}\bigg)\text{,}

implies that the associated operator is positive semidefinite from the observation that taking the constant
C_{N\mathrm{XOR}}
, in the normalization,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\bigg(C_{N\mathrm{XOR}}\omega_{N\mathrm{XOR}}\bigg)n\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq N-1}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)}\text{, }

to equal
N!
implies the desired result, from which we conclude the argument.

\mathrm{XOR}
error bounds, the summations over the list of possible questions,
\mathcal{Q}_{1},\cdots,\mathcal{Q}_{N}
, for each player are excluded; the inequalities are obtained by permuting the order in which the ”interchange” operation of tensor observables of each player is applied. From the optimal value of winning the
N

\mathrm{XOR}
game,

\displaystyle\omega_{N\mathrm{XOR}}\big(G\big)\equiv\omega\big(N\mathrm{XOR}\big)\text{, }

an inequality of the form,

\displaystyle\bigg|\bigg|\bigg[\bigg(\bigg(\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}A^{j_{i}}_{i}\bigg)\bigotimes B_{kl}\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-2}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)-\omega_{N\mathrm{XOR}}\bigg(\pm\bigg(\mathrm{sign}\big(i_{1},j_{1},\cdots,j_{n}\big)\bigg[\bigg(\bigg(\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}A^{j_{i}}_{i}\bigg)

\displaystyle+\bigg(\underset{i+1\equiv i\oplus 1}{\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}}A^{j_{i}}_{i}\bigg)\bigg)\bigg]\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)\bigg)\bigg]\ket{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}}}\bigg|\bigg|\text{, }

\displaystyle\bigg|\bigg|\bigg[\bigg(\bigg(A_{i}\bigotimes\textbf{I}\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{i\in\mathcal{Q}_{1},j\in\mathcal{Q}_{2},k\in\mathcal{Q}_{3}}{\prod}C^{l_{ijk}}_{ijk}\bigg)\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-3}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)-\omega_{N\mathrm{XOR}}\bigg(\pm\mathrm{sign}\big(i_{1},j_{1},k_{1},\cdots,i_{111}

\displaystyle,\cdots,j_{nm(n+m)},\cdots,k_{nm(n+m)}\big)\bigg[\bigg(\textbf{I}\bigotimes\textbf{I}\bigotimes\bigg(\bigg(\underset{k\in\mathcal{Q}_{3},\mathcal{Q}_{3}+1\equiv\mathcal{Q}_{3}\oplus 1}{\underset{j\in\mathcal{Q}_{2},\mathcal{Q}_{2}+1\equiv\mathcal{Q}_{2}\oplus 1}{\underset{i\in\mathcal{Q}_{1},\mathcal{Q}_{1}+1\equiv\mathcal{Q}_{1}\oplus 1}{\prod}}}C^{l_{ijk}}_{ijk}\bigg)+\bigg(\underset{i\in\mathcal{Q}_{1},j\in\mathcal{Q}_{2},k\in\mathcal{Q}_{3}}{\prod}C^{l_{ijk}}_{ijk}\bigg)\bigg)

\displaystyle\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-3}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)\bigg]\bigg)\bigg]\ket{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}}}\bigg|\bigg|\text{, }

\displaystyle\bigg|\bigg|\bigg[\bigg(A_{i}\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq N-2}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{i\in\mathcal{Q}_{1},j\in\mathcal{Q}_{2},k\in\mathcal{Q}_{3}}{\prod}C^{(N-2),l_{ijk}}_{ijk}\bigg)\bigg)-\omega_{N\mathrm{XOR}}\bigg(\pm\mathrm{sign}\big(i_{1},j_{1},k_{1},\cdots,i_{111},\cdots

\displaystyle,j_{nm(n+m)},\cdots,k_{nm(n+m)}\big)\bigg[\bigg(\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq N-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigotimes\bigg(\bigg(\underset{i\in\mathcal{Q}_{1},j\in\mathcal{Q}_{2},k\in\mathcal{Q}_{3}}{\prod}C^{(N-2),l_{ijk}}_{ijk}\bigg)\bigg)

\displaystyle+\bigg(\underset{k\in\mathcal{Q}_{3},\mathcal{Q}_{3}+1\equiv\mathcal{Q}_{3}\oplus 1}{\underset{j\in\mathcal{Q}_{2},\mathcal{Q}_{2}+1\equiv\mathcal{Q}_{2}\oplus 1}{\underset{i\in\mathcal{Q}_{1},\mathcal{Q}_{1}+1\equiv\mathcal{Q}_{1}\oplus 1}{\prod}}}C^{(N-2),l_{ijk}}_{ijk}\bigg)\bigg)\bigg)\bigg]\bigg)\bigg]\ket{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}}}\bigg|\bigg|\text{, }

are expected to hold. The bias for
N
players in the
\mathrm{XOR}
setting,

\displaystyle\beta_{N\mathrm{XOR}}\big(G\big)\equiv\beta\big(N\mathrm{XOR}\big)\text{, }

satisfies,

\displaystyle\big(1-\epsilon_{N\mathrm{XOR}}\big)\beta_{N\mathrm{XOR}}\big(G\big)\leq\underset{\mathcal{Q}_{1},\cdots,\mathcal{Q}_{n}}{\sum}\bra{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}}}\bigg(\underset{\#\text{ players}}{\bigotimes}\text{Tensors of player observables}\bigg)\ket{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}}}

\displaystyle\leq\beta_{N\mathrm{XOR}}\big(G\big)\text{, }

for
\epsilon_{N\mathrm{XOR}}
sufficiently small. Furthermore, from the
N
player primal feasible solution to the associated semidefinite program, one also has,

\displaystyle\big(1-\epsilon_{N\mathrm{XOR}}\big)\omega_{N\mathrm{XOR}}\big(G\big)\leq\underset{\mathcal{Q}_{1},\cdots,\mathcal{Q}_{n}}{\sum}\bra{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}}}\bigg(\underset{\#\text{ players}}{\bigotimes}\text{Tensors of player observables}\bigg)\ket{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}}}

\displaystyle\leq\omega_{N\mathrm{XOR}}\big(G\big)\text{. }

2.3.1
Frobenius norm upper bounds

As a generalization of the superposition that was previously provided for the optimal value,

\displaystyle\omega\big(3\mathrm{XOR}\big)\equiv\omega_{3\mathrm{XOR}}\big(G\big)\text{, }

from the
N
-player bias introduced in the previous section, which satisfies the proportionality,

\displaystyle\omega\big(N\mathrm{XOR}\big)\propto\bigg[\ket{ijklm}\ket{ijk}\bigg(\ket{i}\bra{ij}\bigg)\bra{ijkl}+\ket{\sigma(i\big)\sigma\big(j\big)\sigma\big(k\big)\sigma\big(l\big)\sigma\big(m\big)}

\displaystyle\times\ket{ijk}\bigg(\ket{i}\bra{ij}\bigg)\bra{ijkl}+\ket{ijklm}\ket{\sigma(i\big)\sigma(j\big)\sigma\big(k\big)}\bigg(\ket{i}\bra{ij}\bigg)\bra{ijkl}+\ket{ijklm}

\displaystyle\times\ket{\sigma\big(i\big)\sigma\big(j\big)\sigma\big(k\big)}\bigg(\ket{i}\bra{ij}\bigg)\bra{ijkl}+\ket{ijklm}\ket{ijk}\bigg(\ket{\sigma\big(i\big)}\bra{ij}\bigg)\bra{ijkl}+\ket{ijklm}

\displaystyle\times\ket{ijk}\bigg(\ket{i}\bra{\sigma\big(i\big)\sigma\big(j\big)}\bigg)\bra{ijkl}+\ket{ijklm}\ket{ijk}\bigg(\ket{i}\bra{ij}\bigg)\bra{\sigma\big(i\big)\sigma\big(j\big)\sigma\big(k\big)\sigma\big(l\big)}

\displaystyle+\ket{\sigma\big(i\big)\sigma\big(j\big)\sigma\big(k\big)\sigma\big(l\big)\sigma\big(m\big)}\ket{\sigma\big(i\big)\sigma\big(j\big)\sigma\big(k\big)}\bigg(\ket{i}\bra{ij}\bigg)\bra{ijkl}

\displaystyle+\ket{\sigma\big(i\big)\sigma\big(j\big)\sigma\big(k\big)\sigma\big(l\big)\sigma\big(m\big)}\ket{\sigma\big(i\big)\sigma\big(j\big)\sigma\big(k\big)}\bigg(\ket{\sigma\big(i\big)}\bra{ij}\bigg)\bra{ijkl}

\displaystyle+\ket{\sigma\big(i\big)\sigma\big(j\big)\sigma\big(k\big)\sigma\big(l\big)\sigma\big(m\big)}\ket{\sigma\big(i\big)\sigma\big(j\big)\sigma\big(k\big)}\bigg(\ket{\sigma\big(i\big)}\bra{\sigma\big(i\big)\sigma\big(j\big)}\bigg)

\displaystyle\times\bra{ijkl}+\ket{\sigma\big(i\big)\sigma\big(j\big)\sigma\big(k\big)\sigma\big(l\big)\sigma\big(m\big)}\ket{\sigma\big(i\big)\sigma\big(j\big)\sigma\big(k\big)}\bigg(\ket{\sigma\big(i\big)}\bra{\sigma\big(i\big)\sigma\big(j\big)}\bigg)

\displaystyle\times\bra{\sigma\big(i\big)\sigma\big(j\big)\sigma\big(k\big)\sigma\big(l\big)}+\ket{ijklm}\ket{\sigma\big(i\big)\sigma\big(j\big)\sigma\big(k\big)}\bigg(\ket{i}\bra{ij}\bigg)\bra{ijkl}

\displaystyle+\ket{ijklm}\ket{\sigma\big(i\big)\sigma\big(j\big)\sigma\big(k\big)}\bigg(\ket{\sigma\big(i\big)}\bra{ij}\bigg)\bra{ijkl}+\ket{ijklm}\ket{\sigma\big(i\big)\sigma\big(j\big)\sigma\big(k\big)}

\displaystyle\times\bigg(\ket{\sigma\big(i\big)}\bra{\sigma\big(i\big)\sigma\big(j\big)}\bigg)\bra{ijkl}+\ket{ijklm}\ket{\sigma\big(i\big)\sigma\big(j\big)\sigma\big(k\big)}\bigg(\ket{\sigma\big(i\big)}\bra{\sigma\big(i\big)\sigma\big(j\big)}\bigg)

\displaystyle\times\bra{\sigma\big(i\big)\sigma\big(j\big)\sigma\big(k\big)\sigma\big(l\big)}+\ket{ijklm}\ket{ijk}\bigg(\ket{\sigma\big(i\big)}\bra{\sigma\big(i\big)\sigma\big(j\big)}\bigg)\bra{ijkl}

\displaystyle+\ket{ijklm}\ket{ijk}\bigg(\ket{\sigma\big(i\big)}\bra{\sigma\big(i\big)\sigma\big(j\big)}\bigg)\bra{\sigma\big(i\big)\sigma\big(j\big)\sigma\big(k\big)\sigma\big(l\big)}+\ket{ijklm}

\displaystyle\times\ket{ijk}\bigg(\ket{i}\bra{\sigma\big(i\big)\sigma\big(j\big)}\bigg)\bra{\sigma\big(i\big)\sigma\big(j\big)\sigma\big(k\big)\sigma\big(l\big)}

\displaystyle+\text{Higher order permutations}\bigg]\text{, }

as an extension of the two-player setting, [37], previous work of the author in [44] demonstrated that upper bounds on the Frobenius norm of the following form hold,

\displaystyle\forall i,\big|\big|\big(A_{i}\otimes\textbf{I}\big)T^{\mathrm{FFL}}-T^{\mathrm{FFL}}\big(\widetilde{A_{i}}\otimes\textbf{I}\big)\big|\big|_{\mathrm{F}}<9n^{2}\sqrt{\epsilon}\big|\big|T^{\mathrm{FFL}}\big|\big|_{\mathrm{F}}\text{, }

\displaystyle\forall j\neq k,\big|\big|\big(\textbf{I}\otimes B_{jk}\big)T^{\mathrm{FFL}}-T^{\mathrm{FFL}}\big(\textbf{I}\otimes\widetilde{B_{jk}}\big)\big|\big|_{\mathrm{F}}<\frac{44}{3}n^{2}\sqrt{\epsilon}\big|\big|T^{\mathrm{FFL}}\big|\big|_{\mathrm{F}}\text{, }

for the suitable linear mapping,

\displaystyle T^{\mathrm{FFL}}:\textbf{C}^{2\lceil\frac{n}{2}\rceil}\otimes\textbf{C}^{2\lceil\frac{n}{2}\rceil}\longrightarrow\textbf{C}^{d_{A}}\otimes\textbf{C}^{d_{B}}\text{.
}

To demonstrate that such an argument for upper bounding the Frobenius norm also holds for
3
, and more, players answering questions drawn from the referee’s probability distribution, observe that it suffices to prove that inequalities of the form,

\displaystyle\underline{\text{Player 1}:}\text{ }\bigg|\bigg|\bigg(A_{i}\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)\mathscr{T}-\mathscr{T}\bigg(\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)

\displaystyle\bigotimes\widetilde{A_{i}}\bigg)\bigg|\bigg|_{F}\text{,}

\displaystyle\underline{\text{Player 2}:}\text{ }\bigg|\bigg|\bigg(\textbf{I}\bigotimes B_{ij}\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-2}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)\mathscr{T}-\mathscr{T}\bigg(\textbf{I}\bigotimes\textbf{I}\bigotimes\widetilde{B_{ij}}

\displaystyle\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-3}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)\bigg|\bigg|_{F}\text{, }

\displaystyle\underline{\text{Player 3}:}\text{ }\bigg|\bigg|\bigg(\textbf{I}\bigotimes\textbf{I }\bigotimes C_{ijk}\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-3}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)\mathscr{T}-\mathscr{T}\bigg(\textbf{I}\bigotimes\textbf{I}\bigotimes\textbf{I}\bigotimes\widetilde{C_{ijk}}

\displaystyle\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-4}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)\bigg|\bigg|_{F}\text{, }

with respect to the Frobenius norm can be upper bounded, for
\mathscr{T}\equiv\mathscr{T}_{N\mathrm{XOR}}
. From the fact that the collection of Frobenius norms for the two-player setting imposes the condition that
j\neq k
for the second player’s tensor observable
B_{jk}
, one choose indices of tensors of player observables above from the index set,

\displaystyle\mathscr{I}\equiv\underset{\#\text{ Players }}{\bigcup}\big\{\text{indices }i\text{: }\text{no }i\text{ are equal in Player }j\text{'s tensor observable}\big\}\text{. }

As is the case for the two-player setting, for. the
N
-player setting, given expressions and properties of the game tensor that have been previously discussed, admits the decomposition of the
symmetric
game tensor, which takes the form

\displaystyle G_{N\mathrm{XOR},\mathrm{Sym}}\equiv G_{\mathrm{Sym}}\equiv\frac{1}{2}\begin{bmatrix}0&G^{\textbf{T}}\\
G&0\end{bmatrix}\equiv\frac{1}{2}\begin{bmatrix}0&G_{N\mathrm{XOR}}^{\textbf{T}}\\
G_{N\mathrm{XOR}}&0\end{bmatrix}\text{.}

Besides the symmetrized game tensor introduced above for the N-player setting, obvious counterparts of
G_{N\mathrm{XOR},\mathrm{Sym}}
are introduced. Such tensors appear in the collection of constraints, from the partial ordering
\succcurlyeq
induced by the positive semidefinite cone,

\displaystyle\underset{1\leq i\leq n^{3}}{\sum}y_{3\mathrm{XOR},i}E_{3\mathrm{XOR},ii}\succcurlyeq G_{3\mathrm{XOR},\mathrm{Sym}}\text{, }

(3XOR, Sym)

\displaystyle\underset{1\leq i\leq n^{4}}{\sum}y_{4\mathrm{XOR},i}E_{4\mathrm{XOR},ii}\succcurlyeq G_{4\mathrm{XOR},\mathrm{Sym}}\text{, }

(4XOR, Sym)

\displaystyle\underset{1\leq i\leq n^{5}}{\sum}y_{5\mathrm{XOR},i}E_{5\mathrm{XOR},ii}\succcurlyeq G_{5\mathrm{XOR},\mathrm{Sym}}.\text{, }

(5XOR, Sym)

\displaystyle\underset{1\leq i\leq n^{N}}{\sum}y_{N\mathrm{XOR},i}E_{N\mathrm{XOR},ii}\succcurlyeq G_{N\mathrm{XOR},\mathrm{Sym}}\text{. }

(NXOR, Sym)

Moreover, besides the definition of the
N
-player symmetric game tensor, the primal feasible solution to the semidefinite program satisfies,

\displaystyle G_{\mathrm{Sym}}\cdot Z=\underset{\mathcal{Q}_{1},\cdots,\mathcal{Q}_{n}}{\sum}G_{N\mathrm{XOR}}\bra{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}}}\bigg(\underset{\#\text{ players}}{\bigotimes}\text{Tensors of player observables}\bigg)\ket{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}}}\text{.}

The dual semidefinite program is,

\displaystyle\underset{\underset{\mathcal{Q}_{1},\cdots,\mathcal{Q}_{i},\cdots,\mathcal{Q}_{n}}{\sum}y_{\mathcal{Q}_{i}}E_{ii}\succcurlyeq G_{\mathrm{Sym}}}{\mathrm{inf}}\bigg(\underset{\mathcal{Q}_{1},\cdots,\mathcal{Q}_{i},\cdots,\mathcal{Q}_{N}}{\sum}y_{\mathcal{Q}_{i}}\bigg)\text{. }

\epsilon
-approximality entails that an inequality of the following form,

\displaystyle\underset{1\leq k\leq n}{\sum}\bigg|\bigg|\bigg(\bigg(\big(V_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}\big)_{k}\cdot A_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}\bigg)\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)\ket{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}}}-\bigg(\textbf{I}\bigotimes\bigg(\big(V_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}\big)_{k}\cdot A_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-2}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)\ket{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}}}\bigg|\bigg|\text{, }

is expected to hold, corresponding to the action of the linear operator
\mathscr{T}
between the tensor observables of the first and second player; similar inequalities for tensor observables of other players participating in the game take the form,

\displaystyle\underset{1\leq k\leq n}{\sum}\bigg|\bigg|\bigg(\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigotimes\bigg(\big(V_{\mathcal{Q}_{n}}\big)_{k}\cdot A_{\mathcal{Q}_{N}}\bigg)\bigg)\ket{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}}}-\bigg(\bigg(\big(V_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}\big)_{k}\cdot A_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)\ket{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}}}\bigg|\bigg|\text{. }

The corresponding inequalities for the three-player
\mathrm{XOR}
game take the form,

\displaystyle\underset{1\leq k\leq n}{\sum}\bigg|\bigg|\bigg(\bigg(\big(V_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}\big)_{k}\cdot A_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}\bigg)\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg)\ket{\psi_{3\mathrm{XOR}}}-\bigg(\textbf{I}\otimes\bigg(\big(V_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}\big)_{k}\cdot A_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}\bigg)\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg)\ket{\psi_{3\mathrm{XOR}}}\bigg|\bigg|\text{, }

\displaystyle\underset{1\leq k\leq n}{\sum}\bigg|\bigg|\bigg(\textbf{I}\otimes\bigg(\big(V_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}\big)_{k}\cdot A_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}\bigg)\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg)\ket{\psi_{3\mathrm{XOR}}}-\bigg(\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\bigg(\big(V_{\mathcal{Q}_{3}}\big)_{k}\cdot A_{\mathcal{Q}_{3}}\bigg)\bigg)\ket{\psi_{3\mathrm{XOR}}}\bigg|\bigg|\text{, }

\displaystyle\underset{1\leq k\leq n}{\sum}\bigg|\bigg|\bigg(\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\bigg(\big(V_{\mathcal{Q}_{3}}\big)_{k}\cdot A_{\mathcal{Q}_{3}}\bigg)\bigg)\ket{\psi_{3\mathrm{XOR}}}-\bigg(\bigg(\big(V_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}\big)_{k}\cdot A_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}\bigg)\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg)\ket{\psi_{3\mathrm{XOR}}}\bigg|\bigg|\text{. }

In the three-player setting, we provide an analog to the following result from [37], specifically
Theorem

6
:

Lemma

\textit{1}^{*}
(
the computation of the Frobenius norm for the anticommutation rule of
T
yields the desired
\sqrt{\epsilon}
approximate upper bound
, [44]). One has that,

\displaystyle\bigg|\bigg|\text{ }\bigg(\text{ }\bigg(\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}A^{j_{i}}_{i}\bigg)\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg)\ket{\psi_{\mathrm{FFL}}}-\bigg[\mathrm{sign}\big(i,j_{1},\cdots,j_{n}\big)\text{ }\bigg(\text{ }\bigg(\underset{i\equiv j_{1}+1,\text{ }\mathrm{set}\text{ }j_{1}+1\equiv j_{1}\oplus 1}{\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}}A^{j_{i}}_{i}\bigg)

\displaystyle\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg)\text{ }\bigg]\ket{\psi_{\mathrm{FFL}}}\bigg|\bigg|_{\mathrm{F}}\text{, }

and,

\displaystyle\bigg|\bigg|\bigg(\text{ }\bigg(\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}A^{j_{i}}_{i}\bigg)\otimes B_{kl}\bigg)\ket{\psi_{\mathrm{FFL}}}-\frac{2}{3}\bigg[\pm\mathrm{sign}\big(i,j_{1},\cdots,j_{n}\big)\bigg(\text{ }\bigg(\underset{i\equiv j_{4}+1,\text{ }\mathrm{set}\text{ }j_{4}+1\equiv j_{4}\oplus 1}{\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}}A^{j_{i}}_{i}\bigg)

\displaystyle\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg)\text{ }\bigg]\ket{\psi_{\mathrm{FFL}}}\bigg|\bigg|_{\mathrm{F}}\text{, }

have upper bounds,
9n^{2}\sqrt{\epsilon}
and
\frac{44}{3}n^{2}\sqrt{\epsilon}
, respectively.

Upper bounding the Frobenius norm from the optimal value of the game, in addition to contributions from each agent participating, can be obtained with the following:

Lemma

1 -N
\mathrm{XOR}
(
computation of the Frobenius norm for the anticommutation rule of
T_{N\mathrm{XOR}}
yields a desired up to constants
\sqrt{\epsilon}
upper bound
). One has that,

\displaystyle\underline{\text{Player $1$}:}\text{ }\bigg|\bigg|\bigg(A_{i}\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)\mathscr{T}-\mathscr{T}\bigg(\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigotimes\widetilde{A_{i}}\bigg)\bigg|\bigg|_{F}

\displaystyle<c_{1}n^{N}\sqrt{\epsilon}\text{,}

\displaystyle\underline{\text{Player $N$}:}\text{ }\bigg|\bigg|\bigg(\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigotimes A^{(n-1)}_{i_{1},\cdots,i_{n-1}}\bigg)\mathscr{T}-\mathscr{T}\bigg(\bigg(\widetilde{A^{(n-1)}_{i_{1},\cdots,i_{n-1}}}\bigotimes

\displaystyle\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)\bigg|\bigg|_{F}<c_{N}n^{N}\sqrt{\epsilon}\text{, }

has the upper bound,

\displaystyle\mathscr{C}\equiv\underset{1\leq i\leq N}{\bigcup}\big\{C_{i}\neq c_{i}\in\textbf{R}:C_{i}\equiv c_{i}\sqrt{\epsilon}\big\}\propto n^{N}\sqrt{\epsilon}\text{. }

Tensor observables from each player are drawn from the index set,

\displaystyle\mathscr{I}\equiv\underset{\#\text{ Players }}{\bigcup}\big\{\text{indices }i\text{: }\text{no }i\text{ are equal in Player }j\text{'s tensor observable}\big\}\text{. }

Proof of Lemma 1 -N
\mathrm{XOR}
. Obtaining the desired collection of upper bounds for the Frobenius norm amounts to upper bounding the each Frobenius norm in the statement of the result above, implying,

\displaystyle\bigg|\bigg|\bigg[\bigg(\bigg(\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}A^{j_{i}}_{i}\bigg)\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)-\bigg(\omega_{N\mathrm{XOR}}\bigg(\pm\mathrm{sign}\big(i_{1},\cdots,i_{n}\big)

\displaystyle\times\bigg(\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}A^{j_{i}}_{i}\bigg)\bigg)\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)\bigg]\ket{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}}}\bigg|\bigg|_{F}

\displaystyle<\bigg(n_{1}+\big(n_{1}+2\big)\omega_{N\mathrm{XOR}}^{-1}\bigg)n^{N}\sqrt{\epsilon}\text{,}

\displaystyle\bigg|\bigg|\bigg[\bigg(\textbf{I}\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}A^{1,j_{i}}_{i}\bigg)\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-2}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)-\bigg(\textbf{I}\bigotimes\bigg(\omega_{N\mathrm{XOR}}

\displaystyle\times\bigg(\pm\mathrm{sign}\big(i_{1},j_{1},\cdots,i_{n},\cdots,j_{n}\big)\bigg(\underset{1\leq i_{2}\leq m}{\underset{1\leq i_{1}\leq n}{\prod}}A^{1,j_{i_{1},i_{2}}}_{i_{1},i_{2}}\bigg)\bigg)\bigg)

\displaystyle\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-2}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)\bigg]\ket{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}}}\bigg|\bigg|_{F}<\bigg(n_{2}+\big(n_{2}+2\big)\omega_{N\mathrm{XOR}}^{-1}\bigg)n^{N}\sqrt{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\bigg|\bigg|\bigg[\bigg(\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}A^{(n-1),j_{i_{1},\cdots,i_{n}}}_{i_{1},\cdots,i_{n}}\bigg)\bigg)-\bigg(\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigotimes\bigg(\omega_{N\mathrm{XOR}}

\displaystyle\times\bigg(\pm\mathrm{sign}\big(i_{1},\cdots,i_{n},j_{1},\cdots,j_{n}\big)\bigg)\bigg(\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}A^{(n-1),j_{i_{1},\cdots,i_{n}}}_{i_{1},\cdots,i_{n}}\bigg)\bigg)\bigg)\bigg]\ket{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}}}\bigg|\bigg|_{F}

\displaystyle<\bigg(n_{N}+\big(n_{N}+2\big)\omega_{N\mathrm{XOR}}^{-1}\bigg)n^{N}\sqrt{\epsilon}\text{, }

for the quantum optimal strategy,

\displaystyle\ket{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}}}\equiv\ket{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}}\big(\mathcal{S}\big)}\equiv\underset{\mathcal{S}}{\mathrm{sup}}\big\{\text{payoff for all players with some quantum strategy }\mathcal{S}\big\}\text{,}

and suitable
n_{i}
. Hence the desired upper bound,

\displaystyle\mathscr{C}\text{, }

for the collection of Frobenius norms over all players of the
\mathrm{XOR}
game takes the form,

\displaystyle\underset{1\leq i\leq N}{\sum}\bigg(n_{i}+\big(n_{i}+2\big)\omega^{-1}_{N\mathrm{XOR}}\bigg)n^{N}\sqrt{\epsilon}\equiv\bigg(\big(n_{1}+\cdots+n_{N}\big)+\big(\big(n_{1}+\cdots+n_{N}\big)+2N\big)

\displaystyle\times\omega^{-1}_{N\mathrm{XOR}}\bigg)n^{N}\sqrt{\epsilon}<\bigg(\big(n_{1}+\cdots+n_{N}\big)+\big(\big(n_{1}+\cdots+n_{N}\big)+2N\big)\bigg)\omega^{-1}_{N\mathrm{XOR}}n^{N}\sqrt{\epsilon}

\displaystyle<\bigg(\big(n_{1}+\cdots+n_{N}\big)\bigg(2+2N\bigg)\bigg)\omega^{-1}_{N\mathrm{XOR}}n^{N}\sqrt{\epsilon}<\bigg(\big(n_{1}+\cdots+n_{N}\big)\bigg(2+2N\bigg)\bigg)

\displaystyle\times\big(\omega^{-1}_{N\mathrm{XOR}}n^{N}\big)^{2}\sqrt{\epsilon}\equiv\bigg(2\big(n_{1}+\cdots+n_{N}\big)\big(\omega^{-1}_{N\mathrm{XOR}}n^{N}\big)^{2}+2N\big(n_{1}+\cdots+n_{N}\big)

\displaystyle\times\big(\omega^{-1}_{N\mathrm{XOR}}n^{N}\big)^{2}\bigg)\sqrt{\epsilon}<2\big(n_{1}+\cdots n_{N}\big)Nn^{N}\sqrt{\epsilon}+2N\big(n_{1}+\cdots+n_{N}\big)Nn^{N}\sqrt{\epsilon}

\displaystyle<5N^{2}n_{1}n^{N}\sqrt{\epsilon}<5\big(Nn^{N}\big)^{2}\sqrt{\epsilon}\equiv\mathscr{C}^{\prime}\text{, }

Setting the upper bound to,

\displaystyle\mathscr{C}\equiv\mathscr{C}^{\prime}\text{, }

yields the desired constant, from which we conclude the argument.

As an immediate consequence to the argument above, the following results imply upper bounds for Frobenius norms of games with less than
N
players that can be obtained through similar computations.

Lemma

1-3\mathrm{XOR}
(
computation of the Frobenius norm for the anticommutation rule of
T_{3\mathrm{XOR}}
yields a desired up to constants
\sqrt{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\underline{\text{Player $1$}:}\text{ }\bigg|\bigg|\bigg(A_{i}\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)\mathscr{T}-\mathscr{T}\bigg(\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigotimes\widetilde{A_{i}}\bigg)\bigg|\bigg|_{F}<c_{1}n^{3}\sqrt{\epsilon}\text{,}

\displaystyle\underline{\text{Player $2$}:}\text{ }\bigg|\bigg|\bigg(\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigotimes A^{(n-1)}_{i_{1},\cdots,i_{n-1}}\bigg)\mathscr{T}-\mathscr{T}\bigg(\bigg(\widetilde{A^{(n-1)}_{i_{1},\cdots,i_{n-1}}}\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)\bigg|\bigg|_{F}

\displaystyle<c_{2}n^{3}\sqrt{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\underline{\text{Player $3$}:}\text{ }\bigg|\bigg|\bigg(\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigotimes A^{(n-1)}_{i_{1},\cdots,i_{n-1}}\bigg)\mathscr{T}-\mathscr{T}\bigg(\bigg(\widetilde{A^{(n-1)}_{i_{1},\cdots,i_{n-1}}}\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)\bigg|\bigg|_{F}

\displaystyle<c_{3}n^{3}\sqrt{\epsilon}\text{, }

has the upper bound,

\displaystyle\mathscr{C}\equiv\underset{1\leq i\leq 3}{\bigcup}\big\{C_{i}\neq c_{i}\in\textbf{R}:C_{i}\equiv c_{i}\sqrt{\epsilon}\big\}\propto n^{N}\sqrt{\epsilon}\text{. }

Tensor observables from each player are drawn from the index set,

\displaystyle\mathscr{I}\equiv\underset{\#\text{ Players }}{\bigcup}\big\{\text{indices }i\text{: }\text{no }i\text{ are equal in Player }j\text{'s tensor observable}\big\}\text{. }

As a related property of the suitable linear operator
\mathscr{T}
, an identity for suitable
N\mathrm{XOR}
operators for,

\displaystyle\bigg(A_{i}\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)\mathscr{T}-\mathscr{T}\bigg(\widetilde{A_{i}}\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)\text{, }

can be obtained so that a counterpart identity from that of the
\mathrm{FFL}
game,

\displaystyle\big(A_{i}\otimes\textbf{I}\big)T_{\mathrm{FFL}}-T_{\mathrm{FFL}}\big(\widetilde{A_{i}}\otimes\textbf{I}\big)\text{, }

from the observation that,

\displaystyle\big(A_{i}T_{\mathrm{FFL}}\otimes T_{\mathrm{FFL}}\big)-\big(T_{\mathrm{FFL}}\widetilde{A_{i}}\otimes T_{\mathrm{FFL}}\big)\equiv\big(A_{i}T_{\mathrm{FFL}}-T_{\mathrm{FFL}}\widetilde{A_{i}}\big)\otimes T_{\mathrm{FFL}}\text{, }

in the case of the

\mathrm{XOR}

\displaystyle\bigg(\big(A_{i}\mathscr{T}\otimes\mathscr{T}\big)-\big(\mathscr{T}\widetilde{A_{i}}\otimes\mathscr{T}\big)\bigg)\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-2}{\bigotimes}\mathscr{T}_{k}\bigg)\equiv\bigg(\big(A_{i}\mathscr{T}-\mathscr{T}\widetilde{A_{i}}\big)\bigotimes\mathscr{T}\bigg)

\displaystyle\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-2}{\bigotimes}\mathscr{T}_{k}\bigg)\text{, }

where,

\displaystyle\mathscr{T}_{k}\equiv\mathscr{T}\text{, }

for every

. As a result, to demonstrate that another desired identity holds besides that which has been argued for in the previous result, it suffices to argue that the following

\mathrm{XOR}

Lemma

2
(
N N-XOR identities from two FFL identities
). One has that,

\displaystyle\bigg(A_{i}\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)\mathscr{T}-\mathscr{T}\bigg(\widetilde{A_{i}}\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)\text{, }

and that,

\displaystyle\bigg(\big(A_{i}\mathscr{T}\otimes\mathscr{T}\big)-\big(\mathscr{T}\widetilde{A_{i}}\otimes\mathscr{T}\big)\bigg)\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-2}{\bigotimes}\mathscr{T}_{k}\bigg)\text{, }

are equal, in which,

\displaystyle\bigg(A_{i}\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)\mathscr{T}-\mathscr{T}\bigg(\widetilde{A_{i}}\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)\equiv\bigg(\big(A_{i}\mathscr{T}-\mathscr{T}\widetilde{A_{i}}\big)\bigotimes\mathscr{T}\bigg)

\displaystyle\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-2}{\bigotimes}\mathscr{T}_{k}\bigg)\text{. }

For the remaining
N-1
players in the
\mathrm{XOR}
game, identities of an analagous form hold.

Proof of Lemma 2
. Straightforwardly, to prove that the first identity holds from the action of the suitable
N
-
\mathrm{XOR}
linear operator, write,

\displaystyle\bigg(A_{i}\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)\bigg[\frac{1}{\sqrt{2^{n}}}\bigg[\underset{(j_{1},\cdots,j_{n})\in\{0,1\}^{n}}{\sum}\bigg(\text{ }\bigg(\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}A^{j_{i}}_{i}\bigg)\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)

\displaystyle\times\ket{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}}}\bra{\widetilde{\psi_{{N\mathrm{XOR}}}}}\bigg(\mathrm{sign}\big(i,j_{1},\cdots,j_{n}\big)\bigg(\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}\widetilde{A^{j_{i}}_{i}}\bigg)\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)^{\dagger}\bigg]\bigg]\text{, }

from which the first identity can be verified from the observation that the above tensor product equals,

\displaystyle A_{i}\bigg[\frac{1}{\sqrt{2^{n}}}\bigg[\underset{(j_{1},\cdots,j_{n})\in\{0,1\}^{n}}{\sum}\bigg(\text{ }\bigg(\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}A^{j_{i}}_{i}\bigg)\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)

\displaystyle\times\ket{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}}}\bra{\widetilde{\psi_{{N\mathrm{XOR}}}}}\bigg(\mathrm{sign}\big(i,j_{1},\cdots,j_{n}\big)\bigg(\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}\widetilde{A^{j_{i}}_{i}}\bigg)\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)^{\dagger}\bigg]\bigg]

\displaystyle\bigotimes\bigg(\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg[\frac{1}{\sqrt{2^{n}}}\bigg[\underset{(j_{1},\cdots,j_{n})\in\{0,1\}^{n}}{\sum}\bigg(\bigg(\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}A^{j_{i}}_{i}\bigg)\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)

\displaystyle\times\ket{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}}}\bra{\widetilde{\psi_{{N\mathrm{XOR}}}}}\bigg(\mathrm{sign}\big(i,j_{1},\cdots,j_{n}\big)\bigg(\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}\widetilde{A^{j_{i}}_{i}}\bigg)\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)^{\dagger}\bigg]\bigg]\bigg)\text{, }

which readily implies that the first half of the desired

-
\mathrm{XOR}
identity holds, namely that,

\displaystyle A_{i}\mathscr{T}\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\mathscr{T}\bigg)\text{. }

For the remaining half of the first identity, to demonstrate that anticommuting the order in which
\mathscr{T}
is applied readily implies that,

\displaystyle-\mathscr{T}\bigg(\widetilde{A_{i}}\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)\text{, }

is the remaining half of the first identity, observe,

\displaystyle\bigg[\frac{1}{\sqrt{2^{n}}}\bigg[\underset{(j_{1},\cdots,j_{n})\in\{0,1\}^{n}}{\sum}\bigg(\text{ }\bigg(\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}A^{j_{i}}_{i}\bigg)\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)\ket{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}}}\bra{\widetilde{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}}}}

\displaystyle\times\bigg(\mathrm{sign}\big(i,j_{1},\cdots,j_{n}\big)\text{ }\bigg(\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}\widetilde{A^{j_{i}}_{i}}\bigg)\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)^{\dagger}\bigg]\bigg]\bigg(\widetilde{A_{i}}\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)\text{, }

which equals,

\displaystyle\bigg[\frac{1}{\sqrt{2^{n}}}\bigg[\underset{(j_{1},\cdots,j_{n})\in\{0,1\}^{n}}{\sum}\bigg(\text{ }\bigg(\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}A^{j_{i}}_{i}\bigg)\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)\ket{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}}}

\displaystyle\times\bra{\widetilde{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}}}}\bigg(\mathrm{sign}\big(i,j_{1},\cdots,j_{n}\big)\bigg(\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}\widetilde{A^{j_{i}}_{i}}\bigg)\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)^{\dagger}\text{ }\bigg]\text{ }\bigg]

\displaystyle\times\text{ }\bigg(\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}\widetilde{A^{j_{i}}_{i}}\bigg)\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)^{\dagger}\text{ }\bigg]\bigg]\text{. }

Hence the above tensor product equals the remaining half of the first identity,

\displaystyle-\mathscr{T}\bigg(\widetilde{A_{i}}\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)\text{,}

from which the the first identity holds by adding the two results together. For the remaining identities in the statement of the result above, besides that which are formulated from the observable of the first player, can be obtained by modifying the entry of the tensor product at which

corresponding to the first half of the

\displaystyle\bigg[\frac{1}{\sqrt{2^{n}}}\bigg[\underset{(j_{1},\cdots,j_{n})\in\{0,1\}^{n}}{\sum}\bigg(\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}A^{(n-1)}_{i_{1},\cdots,i_{n}}\bigg)\bigg)\ket{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}}}\bra{\widetilde{\psi_{{N\mathrm{XOR}}}}}

3 3-XOR identities from two FFL identities
). One has that,

The following three variants of the identities, from the group of identities for the

Lemma

2^{**}
(
N N-XOR identities
). One has that,

\displaystyle\bigg(A_{i}\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq z\leq N-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{z}\bigg)\bigg)\mathscr{T}-\mathscr{T}\bigg(A_{i}\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq z\leq N-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{z}\bigg)\bigg)\text{, }

Lemma

2^{***}
(
N N-XOR identities under strong parallel repetition
). One has that,

\displaystyle\bigg(A_{i}\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq z\leq N-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{z}\bigg)\bigg)\bigg(\mathscr{T}\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathscr{T}\bigg)-\bigg(\mathscr{T}\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathscr{T}\bigg)\bigg(A_{i}\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq z\leq N-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{z}\bigg)\bigg)\text{, }

and that,

\displaystyle\bigg(\bigg(A_{i}\bigg(\mathscr{T}\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathscr{T}\bigg)\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq z\leq N-1}{\bigotimes}\bigg(\mathscr{T}\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathscr{T}\bigg)\textbf{I}_{z}\bigg)-\bigg(\bigg(\mathscr{T}\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathscr{T}\bigg)\widetilde{A_{i}}

\displaystyle\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq z\leq N-1}{\bigotimes}\bigg(\mathscr{T}\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathscr{T}\bigg)\textbf{I}_{z}\bigg)\bigg)\bigg)\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq z\leq N}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{z}\bigg)\text{, }

are equal, in which,

\displaystyle\bigg(A_{i}\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq z\leq N-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{z}\bigg)\bigg)\bigg(\mathscr{T}\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathscr{T}\bigg)-\bigg(\mathscr{T}\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathscr{T}\bigg)\bigg(\widetilde{A_{i}}\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq z\leq N-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{z}\bigg)\bigg)

\displaystyle\equiv\bigg(\bigg(A_{i}\bigg(\mathscr{T}\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathscr{T}\bigg)-\bigg(\mathscr{T}\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathscr{T}\bigg)\widetilde{A_{i}}\bigg)\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq z\leq n-1}{\bigotimes}\bigg(\mathscr{T}\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathscr{T}\bigg)\textbf{I}_{z}\bigg)\bigg)

\displaystyle\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq z\leq N-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{z}\bigg)\text{. }

Proof of Lemma
2^{**}
, and
2^{***}
. Directly apply the arguments from the previous result, from which we conclude the argument.

With the previous set of results,
\epsilon
-optimality of the
N
-
\mathrm{XOR}

\mathrm{FFL}

Lemma

\textit{3}^{*}
(
\epsilon
-optimality
,
Lemma

7
, [44]). For an
\epsilon
-optimal strategy
A_{i}
,
B_{jk}
and
\ket{\psi_{\mathrm{FFL}}}
,

\displaystyle\underset{1\leq i<j\leq n}{\sum}\bigg|\bigg|\bigg(\bigg(\frac{A_{i}A_{j}+A_{j}A_{i}}{2}\bigg)\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg)\ket{\psi_{\mathrm{FFL}}}\bigg|\bigg|^{2}<2\big(\frac{7}{3}\big)^{2}n\big(n-1\big)\epsilon\text{. }

In game theoretic settings with more than two active players, inequalities of the form indicated below are expected to hold.

Lemma

3
(
intialization of
\epsilon
-optimality of the N
\mathrm{XOR}
game from the observable of the first player
). For an
\epsilon
-optimal strategy, and player observable tensors
A_{i_{1}},A^{1}_{i_{1},i_{2}},\cdots,A^{n-1}_{i_{1},i_{2},\cdots,i_{n}}
, and
\ket{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}}}
, and
C_{1}>0
,

\displaystyle\underset{i,j}{\sum}\bigg|\bigg|\bigg[\bigg(\bigg(\frac{A_{i}A_{j}+A_{j}A_{i}}{2}\bigg)\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq N-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)\bigg]\ket{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}}}\bigg|\bigg|^{2}<C_{1}n\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq N-1}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)\epsilon_{N\mathrm{XOR}}\text{. }

Proof of Lemma 3
. By direct computation, along the lines of a previous adaptation from the two-player setting for
\mathrm{XOR}^{*}
and
\mathrm{FFL}
games in [44],

\displaystyle 2\underset{1\leq i<j\leq n}{\sum}\bigg|\bigg|\bigg(\bigg(\frac{A_{i}A_{j}+A_{j}A_{i}}{2}\bigg)\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq N-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)\ket{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}}}\bigg|\bigg|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\big(1+\omega^{-1}_{N\mathrm{XOR}}\big)^{2}\underset{1\leq i<j\leq n}{\sum}\bigg[\bigg|\bigg|\bigg(\bigg(\frac{A_{i}+A_{j}}{\sqrt{2}}\bigg)\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq N-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)\ket{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}}}

\displaystyle-\bigg(\textbf{I}\bigotimes B_{ij}\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq N-2}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)\times\ket{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}}}\bigg|\bigg|^{2}+\bigg|\bigg|\bigg(\bigg(\frac{A_{i}-A_{j}}{\sqrt{2}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq N-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)\ket{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}}}-\bigg(\textbf{I}\bigotimes B_{ji}\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq N-2}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)

\displaystyle\times\ket{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}}}\bigg|\bigg|^{2}\bigg]\text{, }

implies the desired upper bound is obtained from the observations,

\displaystyle\underset{1\leq i<j\leq n}{\sum}\bigg|\bigg|\bigg(\bigg(\frac{A_{i}+A_{j}}{\sqrt{2}}\bigg)\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq N-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)\ket{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}}}-\bigg(\textbf{I}\bigotimes B_{ij}\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq N-2}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)

\displaystyle\times\ket{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}}}\bigg|\bigg|^{2}<\frac{1}{100}n\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq N-1}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)\epsilon_{N\mathrm{XOR}}\text{, }

and also that,

\displaystyle\underset{1\leq i<j\leq n}{\sum}\bigg|\bigg|\bigg(\bigg(\frac{A_{i}-A_{j}}{\sqrt{2}}\bigg)\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq N-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)\ket{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}}}-\bigg(\textbf{I}\bigotimes B_{ji}\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq N-2}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)

\displaystyle\times\ket{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}}}\bigg|\bigg|^{2}<\frac{1}{100}n\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq N-1}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)\epsilon_{N\mathrm{XOR}}\text{, }

which together yield the desired upper bound, upon taking constants
C_{1}
satisfying,

\displaystyle C_{1}>\frac{1}{50}\text{, }

from the observation that the desired upper bound holds iff,

\displaystyle\underset{i,j}{\sum}\bigg|\bigg|\bigg[\bigg(\bigg(\frac{A_{i}A_{j}+A_{j}A_{i}}{2}\bigg)\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq N-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)\bigg]\ket{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}}}\bigg|\bigg|^{2}

\displaystyle<\frac{1}{50}n\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq N-1}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)\epsilon_{\mathrm{XOR}}\Longleftrightarrow C_{1}>\frac{1}{50}\text{, }

from which we conclude the argument.

From the previous results, given the optimal value of a game,

\displaystyle\text{Classical Optimal Value}\equiv\underset{\mathcal{S}}{\mathrm{sup}}\big\{\text{Probability that a player wins the game with some}

\displaystyle\text{classical strategy }\mathcal{S}\big\}\text{, }

the two-player game, given the tensor observables of each player, satisfies:

Besides the observable gathered by the first player, the bound straightforwardly adapted in the multiplayer
\mathrm{XOR}
setting from [44], the upper bound for simultaneously applying operations to change the order in which player observables appear in tensor products takes the following form.

Lemma

4A
(
induction on the, up to constants,
\epsilon
upper bound from the previous result
). Under the assumptions of
Lemma

3
,

\displaystyle\underset{\text{Tensor entries }}{\sum}\bigg[\underset{i_{1},\cdots,i_{n}}{\sum}\bigg|\bigg|\bigg[\bigg(\bigg(\frac{A_{i}A_{j}+A_{j}A_{i}}{2}\bigg)\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq N-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)\bigg]\ket{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}}}\bigg|\bigg|^{2}\bigg]

\displaystyle<n^{N}\bigg(\bigg(\underset{1\leq z\leq N}{\bigotimes}\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq N-1}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)\textbf{I}_{z}\bigg)\epsilon_{N\mathrm{XOR}}\bigg)\text{. }

Proof of Lemma 4A
. Induction on the, up to constants,
\epsilon
upper bound in the previous result implies that the desired upper bound, which we denote with
C_{2}
, takes the form,

\displaystyle C_{2}\equiv\bigg(n\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq N-1}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)\textbf{I}\bigg)\epsilon_{N\mathrm{XOR}}\bigotimes\overset{N-2}{\cdots}\bigotimes\bigg(n\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq N-1}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)\textbf{I}\bigg)\epsilon_{N\mathrm{XOR}}

\displaystyle>\bigg(\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq N-1}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)\textbf{I}\bigg)\epsilon_{N\mathrm{XOR}}\bigotimes\overset{N-2}{\cdots}\bigotimes\bigg(\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq N-1}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)\textbf{I}\bigg)\epsilon_{N\mathrm{XOR}}

\displaystyle\underset{1\leq z\leq N-1}{\bigotimes}\bigg(\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq N-1}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)\textbf{I}_{z}\bigg)\epsilon_{N\mathrm{XOR}}

\displaystyle>\underset{\text{Tensor entries}}{\sum}\bigg[\underset{i,j}{\sum}\bigg|\bigg|\bigg[\bigg(\bigg(\frac{A_{i}A_{j}+A_{j}A_{i}}{2}\bigg)\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq N-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)\bigg]\ket{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}}}\bigg|\bigg|^{2}\bigg]\text{, }

from which we conclude the argument, as,

\displaystyle n^{N}\bigg(\underset{1\leq z\leq N}{\bigotimes}\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq N-1}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)\textbf{I}_{z}\bigg)\epsilon_{N\mathrm{XOR}}\bigg)\equiv C_{2}\text{. }\boxed{}

Lemma

\textit{4A}^{*}
(
induction on the, up to constants, strong parallel repetition
\epsilon
upper bound from the previous result
). Under the assumptions of
Lemma

4A
, and
Lemma

3
, and
C^{\wedge}_{2}>0
,

\displaystyle\underset{\text{Tensor entries }}{\sum}\bigg[\underset{j^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,j^{\prime}_{n}}{\underset{i^{\prime},\cdots,i^{\prime}_{n}}{\underset{i_{1},\cdots,i_{n}}{\sum}}}\bigg|\bigg|\bigg[\bigg(\bigg(\frac{\big(A_{i}\wedge A_{i^{\prime}}\big)\big(A_{j}\wedge A_{j^{\prime}}\big)+\big(A_{j}\wedge A_{j^{\prime}}\big)\big(A_{i}\wedge A_{i^{\prime}}\big)}{2}\bigg)\bigotimes\textbf{I}\bigg)\bigg]\ket{\psi_{2\mathrm{XOR}}}\bigg|\bigg|^{2}\bigg]

\displaystyle<C^{\wedge}_{2}n\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq 2}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)\epsilon_{2\mathrm{XOR}\wedge 2\mathrm{XOR}}\equiv C^{\wedge}_{2}n\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq 2}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)\epsilon_{\mathrm{XOR}\wedge\mathrm{XOR}}\text{. }

Proof of Lemma
4A^{*}
. Directly apply the arguments from the previous result, from which we conclude the argument.

In the
N
-player setting, the strong parallel repetition result above takes the expected form, given below:

Lemma

\textit{4A}^{**}
(
induction on the, up to constants,
N
-player strong parallel repetition
\epsilon
upper bound from the previous result
). Under the assumptions of
Lemma

4A
,
Lemma

\textit{4A}^{*}
, and
Lemma

3
, and
C^{\wedge}_{N}>0
,

\displaystyle\underset{\text{Tensor entries }}{\sum}\bigg[\underset{i^{\prime\cdots\prime},\cdots,i^{\prime\cdots\prime}}{\underset{\vdots}{\underset{i_{1},\cdots,i_{n}}{\sum}}}\bigg|\bigg|\bigg[\bigg(\bigg(\frac{\big(A_{i}\wedge A_{i^{\prime}}\wedge\cdots\wedge A_{i^{\prime\cdots\prime}}\big)\big(A_{j}\wedge A_{j^{\prime}}\wedge\cdots\wedge A_{j^{\prime\cdots\prime}}\big)+\big(A_{j}\wedge A_{j^{\prime}}\wedge\cdots\wedge A_{j^{\prime\cdots\prime}}\big)}{2}

\displaystyle\times\frac{\cdots\big(A_{i}\wedge A_{i^{\prime}}\wedge\cdots\wedge A_{i^{\prime\cdots\prime}}\big)}{\cdots}\bigg)\bigotimes\textbf{I}\bigg)\bigg]\ket{\psi_{\mathrm{XOR}\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathrm{XOR}}}\bigg|\bigg|^{2}\bigg]<C^{\wedge}_{N}n\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq N}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)\epsilon_{N\mathrm{XOR}\wedge\cdots\wedge N\mathrm{XOR}}

\displaystyle\equiv C^{\wedge}_{N}n\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq N}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)\epsilon_{\mathrm{XOR}\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathrm{XOR}}\text{. }

Proof of Lemma
4A^{**}
. Directly apply the arguments from the previous two results, from which we conclude the argument.

We conclude the series of related results for strong parallel repetition of the
\mathrm{FFL}
game, which can also be shown to hold using identical arguments.

Lemma

\textit{4A}^{***}
(
induction on the, up to constants,
\mathrm{FFL}
strong parallel repetition
\epsilon
upper bound from the previous result
). Under the assumptions of
Lemma

4A
, and
Lemma

3
, and
C^{\wedge}_{2}>0
,

\displaystyle\underset{\text{Tensor entries }}{\sum}\bigg[\underset{i^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,i^{\prime}_{n}}{\underset{i_{1},\cdots,i_{n}}{\sum}}\bigg|\bigg|\bigg[\bigg(\bigg(\frac{\big(A_{i}\wedge A_{i^{\prime}}\big)\big(A_{j}\wedge A_{j^{\prime}}\big)+\big(A_{j}\wedge A_{j^{\prime}}\big)\big(A_{i}\wedge A_{i^{\prime}}\big)}{2}\bigg)\bigotimes\textbf{I}\bigg)\bigg]\ket{\psi_{\mathrm{FFL}}}\bigg|\bigg|^{2}\bigg]

\displaystyle<C^{\wedge}_{2}n\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq 2}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)\epsilon_{\mathrm{FFL}\wedge\mathrm{FFL}}\text{. }

Proof of Lemma
4A^{***}
. Directly apply the arguments from the previous three results, from which we conclude the argument.

In comparison to upper bounds that are only dependent upon
\epsilon
, other results for applying transformations to observables of one player, have the following impact on the observables of the other place for two players:

Lemma

4B
(
\sqrt{\epsilon}
-
approximality
,
Lemma

8
, [44]). From the same quantities introduced in the previous result, one has,

\displaystyle\bigg|\bigg|\bigg(A_{k}\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg)\ket{\psi_{\mathrm{FFL}}}-\bigg(\textbf{I}\otimes\bigg(\frac{\pm B_{kl}+B_{lk}}{\big|\pm B_{kl}+B_{lk}\big|}\bigg)\bigg)\ket{\psi_{\mathrm{FFL}}}\bigg|\bigg|<17\sqrt{n\epsilon}\text{. }

In addition to the result above, the following result below is used to characterize the error bound resulting from permuting the indicates of the first player’s tensor observable.

Lemma

5
(
error bound from permuting indices
,
Lemma

5
, [44]). One has,

\displaystyle\bigg|\bigg|\bigg(\bigg(\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}A^{j_{i}}_{i}\bigg)-\bigg(\underset{\text{if }i\equiv j_{1}+1,\text{ }\mathrm{set}\text{ }j_{1}+1\equiv j_{1}\oplus 1}{\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}}A^{j_{i}}_{i}\bigg)\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg)\ket{\psi_{\mathrm{FFL}}}\bigg|\bigg|\leq\frac{100}{9}n^{2}\sqrt{\epsilon}\text{. }

We postpone the arguments for generalizing
Lemma

4B
to
1.5.3
, due to the fact that more complicated upper bounds must be computed, under several circumstances, for games with more than two players. In comparison to several technical computations that must be employed for demonstrating that counterparts of
Lemma

4B
, above, hold,
Lemma

5
can be shown to hold from previous observations relating to the optimal values of the
\mathrm{XOR}
, and
\mathrm{FFL}
games,

\displaystyle\text{Quantum }\mathrm{XOR/XOR}^{*}\text{ value}\equiv\omega\big(\mathrm{XOR}\big)\equiv\omega\big(\mathrm{XOR}^{*}\big)\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\text{, }

\displaystyle\text{Classical }\mathrm{FFL}\text{ value}\equiv\text{Quantum }\mathrm{FFL}\text{ value}\equiv\omega\big(\mathrm{FFL}\big)\equiv\frac{2}{3}\text{, }

in addition to whether the optimal value of the game changes under strong parallel repetition, in which,

\displaystyle\omega_{\mathrm{XOR}\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathrm{XOR}}\big(G\big)\equiv\omega\big(\mathrm{XOR}\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathrm{XOR}\big)\equiv\underset{\#\text{ of strong parallel repetitions }j}{\prod}\omega\big(\mathrm{XOR}\big)^{j}

\displaystyle\equiv\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\bigg)^{\#\text{ of strong parallel repetitions }}\equiv\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\bigg)^{n}\text{, }

\displaystyle\omega_{\mathrm{FFL}\wedge\mathrm{FFL}}\big(G\big)\equiv\frac{2}{3}\neq\underset{\#\text{ of strong parallel repetitions }j}{\prod}\omega\big(\mathrm{XOR}\big)^{j}

\displaystyle\equiv\bigg(\frac{2}{3}\bigg)^{\#\text{ of strong parallel repetitions}}\neq\omega_{\mathrm{FFL}}\big(G\big)\equiv\frac{2}{3}\text{. }

We make use of the two properties of the
\mathrm{XOR}
and
\mathrm{FFL}
optimal values below.

Lemma

\textit{5}^{*}
(
error bound from permuting indices in the N-player setting
,
Lemma

5
, [44]). One has the following error bound from permuting indices,

\displaystyle\bigg|\bigg|\bigg(\bigg(\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}A^{j_{i}}_{i}\bigg)\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq z\leq N-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{z}\bigg)\bigg)\ket{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}}}-\bigg(\bigg(\underset{\text{if }i\equiv j_{1}+1,\text{ }\mathrm{set}\text{ }j_{1}+1\equiv j_{1}\oplus 1}{\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}}A^{j_{i}}_{i}\bigg)

\displaystyle\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq z\leq N-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{z}\bigg)\bigg)\times\ket{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}}}\bigg|\bigg|<Nn^{N+\epsilon_{N\mathrm{XOR}}}\omega^{3}_{N\mathrm{XOR}}\text{. }

Proof of Lemma
5^{*}
. Set
\epsilon\equiv\epsilon_{N\mathrm{XOR}}
. The desired upper bound is of the form,

\displaystyle n^{N}\sqrt{\epsilon}\bigg(\bigg(\omega_{N\mathrm{XOR}}^{-2}+\omega_{N\mathrm{XOR}}^{-1}\bigg)+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n^{N-1}}}\bigg(\omega_{N\mathrm{XOR}}^{-2}+\omega_{N\mathrm{XOR}}^{-1}\bigg)\bigg)

\displaystyle\equiv n^{N}\sqrt{\epsilon}\bigg(\omega_{N\mathrm{XOR}}^{-2}+\omega_{N\mathrm{XOR}}^{-1}\bigg)+n^{N}\sqrt{\epsilon}\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n^{N-1}}}\bigg(\omega_{N\mathrm{XOR}}^{-2}+\omega_{N\mathrm{XOR}}^{-1}\bigg)\bigg)

\displaystyle<n^{N}\sqrt{\epsilon}\bigg(\omega_{N\mathrm{XOR}}^{2}+\omega_{N\mathrm{XOR}}\bigg)+n^{N}\sqrt{\epsilon}\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n^{N-1}}}\bigg(\omega_{N\mathrm{XOR}}^{2}+\omega_{N\mathrm{XOR}}\bigg)\bigg)\text{, }

which can further be rearranged as to obtain the desired upper bound,

\displaystyle n^{N}\sqrt{\epsilon}\bigg(\omega_{N\mathrm{XOR}}^{2}+\omega_{N\mathrm{XOR}}\bigg)+n^{N}\sqrt{\epsilon}\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n^{N-1}}}\bigg(\omega_{N\mathrm{XOR}}^{2}+\omega_{N\mathrm{XOR}}\bigg)\bigg)

\displaystyle\equiv n^{N}\sqrt{\epsilon}\bigg[\bigg(\omega_{N\mathrm{XOR}}^{2}+\omega_{N\mathrm{XOR}}\bigg)+\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n^{N-1}}}\bigg(\omega_{N\mathrm{XOR}}^{2}+\omega_{N\mathrm{XOR}}\bigg)\bigg)\bigg]

\displaystyle<n^{N}\sqrt{\epsilon}\omega_{N\mathrm{XOR}}^{3}\bigg[1+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n^{N-1}}}\bigg]

\displaystyle<n^{N+\epsilon}\omega^{3}_{N\mathrm{XOR}}\bigg[1+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n^{N-1}}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\equiv n^{N+\epsilon}\omega^{3}_{N\mathrm{XOR}}+\frac{n^{N+\epsilon}\omega^{3}_{N\mathrm{XOR}}}{\sqrt{n^{N-1}}}

\displaystyle<n^{N+\epsilon}\omega^{3}_{N\mathrm{XOR}}+n^{N+\epsilon}\omega^{3}_{N\mathrm{XOR}}

\displaystyle\equiv 2n^{N+\epsilon}\omega^{3}_{N\mathrm{XOR}}

\displaystyle<Nn^{N+\epsilon}\omega^{3}_{N\mathrm{XOR}}\text{, }

from which we conclude the argument.

Lemma

\textit{5}^{**}
(
error bound from permuting indices in the strong parallel repetition of the N-player setting
,
Lemma

5
, [44]). One has the following error bound from permuting indices,

\displaystyle\bigg|\bigg|\bigg(\bigg(\bigg(\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}A^{j_{i}}_{i}\bigg)\wedge\cdots\wedge\bigg(\underset{1\leq i^{\prime\cdots\prime}\leq n^{\prime\cdots\prime}}{\prod}A^{j^{\prime\cdots\prime}_{i^{\prime\cdots\prime}}}_{i^{\prime\cdots\prime}}\bigg)\bigg)\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq z\leq N-1}{\bigotimes}\big(\textbf{I}_{z}\wedge\cdots\wedge\textbf{I}_{z}\big)\bigg)\bigg)

\displaystyle\times\ket{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}\wedge\cdots\wedge N\mathrm{XOR}}}

\displaystyle-\bigg(\bigg(\bigg(\underset{\text{if }i\equiv j_{1}+1,\text{ }\mathrm{set}\text{ }j_{1}+1\equiv j_{1}\oplus 1}{\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}}A^{j_{i}}_{i}\bigg)\wedge\cdots\wedge\bigg(\underset{\text{if }i^{\prime\cdots\prime}\equiv j^{\prime\cdots\prime}_{1}+1,\text{ }\mathrm{set}\text{ }j^{\prime\cdots\prime}_{1}+1\equiv j^{\prime\cdots\prime}_{1}\oplus 1}{\underset{1\leq i^{\prime\cdots\prime}\leq n^{\prime\cdots\prime}}{\prod}}A^{j^{\prime\cdots\prime}_{i^{\prime\cdots\prime}}}_{i^{\prime\cdots\prime}}\bigg)\bigg)

\displaystyle\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq z\leq N-1}{\bigotimes}\big(\textbf{I}_{z}\wedge\cdots\wedge\textbf{I}_{z}\big)\bigg)\bigg)\ket{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}\wedge\cdots\wedge N\mathrm{XOR}}}\bigg|\bigg|<n^{N+\epsilon}_{\wedge}+\bigg(\frac{50n^{N+\epsilon}_{\wedge}}{\sqrt{n^{N-1}}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\times\omega_{N\mathrm{XOR}\wedge\cdots\wedge N\mathrm{XOR}}\text{. }

Proof of Lemma
5^{**}
. Set
\epsilon\equiv\epsilon_{N\mathrm{XOR}\wedge\cdots\wedge N\mathrm{XOR}}
. The desired upper bound is of the form,

\displaystyle n^{N}_{\wedge}\sqrt{\epsilon}\bigg(\bigg(\omega_{N\mathrm{XOR}\wedge\cdots\wedge N{\mathrm{XOR}}}^{-2}+\omega_{N\mathrm{XOR}\wedge\cdots\wedge N{\mathrm{XOR}}}^{-1}\bigg)+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n^{N-1}}}\bigg(\omega_{N\mathrm{XOR}\wedge\cdots\wedge N{\mathrm{XOR}}}^{-2}

\displaystyle+\omega_{N\mathrm{XOR}\wedge\cdots\wedge N{\mathrm{XOR}}}^{-1}\bigg)\bigg)

\displaystyle\equiv n^{N}_{\wedge}\sqrt{\epsilon}\bigg(\bigg(\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\bigg)^{-2N}+\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\bigg)^{-N}\bigg)+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n^{N-1}}}\bigg(\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\bigg)^{-2N}+\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\bigg)^{-N}\bigg)\bigg)

\displaystyle\equiv\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\bigg)^{-2N}\bigg[n^{N}_{\wedge}\sqrt{\epsilon}\bigg(\bigg(1+\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\bigg)^{N}\bigg)+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n^{N-1}}}\bigg(1+\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\bigg)^{N}\bigg)\bigg)\bigg]

\displaystyle<\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\bigg)^{-2N}\bigg[n^{N+\epsilon}_{\wedge}\bigg(\bigg(1+\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\bigg)^{N}\bigg)+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n^{N-1}}}\bigg(1+\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\bigg)^{N}\bigg)\bigg)\bigg]

\displaystyle<\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\bigg)^{-N}\bigg[n^{N+\epsilon}_{\wedge}\bigg(\bigg(1+\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\bigg)^{N}\bigg)+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n^{N-1}}}\bigg(1+\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\bigg)^{N}\bigg)\bigg)\bigg]

\displaystyle<\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\bigg)^{-N}\bigg[n^{N+\epsilon}_{\wedge}\bigg(\bigg(\frac{2}{\sqrt{2}}\bigg)^{N}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n^{N-1}}}\bigg(\frac{2}{\sqrt{2}}\bigg)^{N}\bigg)\bigg]

\displaystyle\equiv\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\bigg)^{-N}\bigg(\frac{2}{\sqrt{2}}\bigg)^{N}\bigg[n^{N+\epsilon}_{\wedge}\bigg(1+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n^{N-1}}}\bigg)\bigg]

\displaystyle<\bigg(\frac{2}{\sqrt{2}}\bigg)^{-N}\bigg(\frac{2}{\sqrt{2}}\bigg)^{N}\bigg[n^{N+\epsilon}_{\wedge}\bigg(1+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n^{N-1}}}\bigg)\bigg]

\displaystyle\equiv\bigg[n^{N+\epsilon}_{\wedge}\bigg(1+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n^{N-1}}}\bigg)\bigg]

\displaystyle<n^{N+\epsilon}_{\wedge}+\bigg(\frac{50n^{N+\epsilon}_{\wedge}}{\sqrt{n^{N-1}}}\bigg)\omega_{N\mathrm{XOR}\wedge\cdots\wedge N\mathrm{XOR}}\text{, }

from which we conclude the argument.

Lemma

\textit{5}^{***}
(
error bound from permuting indices in the strong parallel repetition of the
\mathrm{FFL}
2-player setting
,
Lemma

5
, [44]). One has the following error bound from permuting indices,

\displaystyle\bigg|\bigg|\bigg(\bigg(\bigg(\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}A^{j_{i}}_{i}\bigg)\wedge\bigg(\underset{1\leq i^{\prime}\leq n^{\prime}}{\prod}A^{j^{\prime}_{i^{\prime}}}_{i^{\prime}}\bigg)\bigg)\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq z\leq N-1}{\bigotimes}\big(\textbf{I}_{z}\wedge\textbf{I}_{z}\big)\bigg)\bigg)

\displaystyle\times\ket{\psi_{\mathrm{FFL}\wedge\mathrm{FFL}}}

\displaystyle-\bigg(\bigg(\bigg(\underset{\text{if }i\equiv j_{1}+1,\text{ }\mathrm{set}\text{ }j_{1}+1\equiv j_{1}\oplus 1}{\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}}A^{j_{i}}_{i}\bigg)\wedge\bigg(\underset{\text{if }i^{\prime}\equiv j^{\prime}_{1}+1,\text{ }\mathrm{set}\text{ }j^{\prime}_{1}+1\equiv j^{\prime}_{1}\oplus 1}{\underset{1\leq i^{\prime}\leq n^{\prime}}{\prod}}A^{j^{\prime}_{i^{\prime}}}_{i^{\prime}}\bigg)\bigg)

\displaystyle\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq z\leq N-1}{\bigotimes}\big(\textbf{I}_{z}\wedge\textbf{I}_{z}\big)\bigg)\bigg)\ket{\psi_{\mathrm{FFL}\wedge\mathrm{FFL}}}\bigg|\bigg|<n^{N+\epsilon}_{\wedge}

\displaystyle+\bigg(\frac{50n^{N+\epsilon}_{\wedge}}{\sqrt{n}}\bigg)\omega_{\mathrm{FFL}\wedge\mathrm{FFL}}\text{. }

Proof of Lemma
5^{***}
. The desired upper bound follows from a variant of that provided in the argument for the previous result, upon setting
\epsilon_{\mathrm{FFL}\wedge\mathrm{FFL}}\equiv\epsilon
,

\displaystyle n^{2}_{\wedge}\sqrt{\epsilon}\bigg(\bigg(\omega_{\mathrm{FFL}\wedge\mathrm{FFL}}^{-2}+\omega_{\mathrm{FFL}\wedge\mathrm{FFL}}^{-1}\bigg)+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\bigg(\omega_{\mathrm{FFL}\wedge\mathrm{FFL}}^{-2}+\omega_{\mathrm{FFL}\wedge\mathrm{FFL}}^{-1}\bigg)\bigg)

\displaystyle\equiv n^{2}_{\wedge}\sqrt{\epsilon}\bigg(\bigg(\bigg(\frac{2}{3}\bigg)^{-4}+\bigg(\frac{2}{3}\bigg)^{-2}\bigg)+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\bigg(\bigg(\frac{2}{3}\bigg)^{-4}+\bigg(\frac{2}{3}\bigg)^{-2}\bigg)\bigg)

\displaystyle\equiv\bigg(\frac{2}{3}\bigg)^{-4}\bigg[n^{N}_{\wedge}\sqrt{\epsilon}\bigg(\bigg(1+\bigg(\frac{2}{3}\bigg)^{2}\bigg)+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\bigg(1+\bigg(\frac{2}{3}\bigg)^{2}\bigg)\bigg)\bigg]

\displaystyle<\bigg(\frac{2}{3}\bigg)^{-4}\bigg[n^{N+\epsilon}_{\wedge}\bigg(\bigg(1+\bigg(\frac{2}{3}\bigg)^{2}\bigg)+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\bigg(1+\bigg(\frac{2}{3}\bigg)^{2}\bigg)\bigg)\bigg]

\displaystyle<\bigg(\frac{2}{3}\bigg)^{-2}\bigg[n^{N+\epsilon}_{\wedge}\bigg(\bigg(1+\bigg(\frac{2}{3}\bigg)^{2}\bigg)+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\bigg(1+\bigg(\frac{2}{3}\bigg)^{2}\bigg)\bigg)\bigg]

\displaystyle<\bigg(\frac{2}{3}\bigg)^{-2}\bigg[n^{N+\epsilon}_{\wedge}\bigg(\bigg(\bigg(\frac{3}{2}\bigg)^{2}+\bigg(\frac{2}{3}\bigg)^{2}\bigg)+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\bigg(\bigg(\frac{3}{2}\bigg)^{2}+\bigg(\frac{2}{3}\bigg)^{2}\bigg)\bigg)\bigg]

\displaystyle<\bigg(\frac{2}{3}\bigg)^{-2}\bigg[n^{N+\epsilon}_{\wedge}\bigg(\bigg(2\bigg(\frac{3}{2}\bigg)^{2}\bigg)+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\bigg(2\bigg(\frac{3}{2}\bigg)^{2}\bigg)\bigg)\bigg]

\displaystyle\equiv\bigg(2\bigg(\frac{3}{2}\bigg)^{2}\bigg)\bigg(\frac{2}{3}\bigg)^{-2}\bigg[n^{N+\epsilon}_{\wedge}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\bigg]

\displaystyle<2\bigg[n^{N+\epsilon}_{\wedge}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\bigg]\equiv 2n^{N+\epsilon}_{\wedge}+\frac{2}{\sqrt{n}}

\displaystyle<n^{N+\epsilon}_{\wedge}+\bigg(\frac{50n^{N+\epsilon}_{\wedge}}{\sqrt{n}}\bigg)\omega_{\mathrm{FFL}\wedge\mathrm{FFL}}\text{, }

from which we conclude the argument.

2.3.2
5
-
\mathrm{XOR}
game

For the
5
-
\mathrm{XOR}
game, introduce,

\displaystyle\underline{\mathscr{P}_{5\mathrm{XOR}}}\equiv\underline{\mathscr{P}_{5\mathrm{XOR},1}}\cup\underline{\mathscr{P}_{5\mathrm{XOR},1,2}}\cup\underline{\mathscr{P}_{5\mathrm{XOR},1,3}}\cup\cdots\cup\underline{\mathscr{P}_{5\mathrm{XOR},1,2,3,4,5}}\text{, }

where each set of permutations
\mathscr{P}
are of the form,

\displaystyle\underline{\mathscr{P}_{5\mathrm{XOR},1}}\equiv\underset{\sigma\in S_{5}}{\bigcup}\big\{\text{Permutations }\sigma\text{ of the first player's tensor observable}\big\}\text{, }

\displaystyle\underline{\mathscr{P}_{5\mathrm{XOR},1,2,3,4,5}}\equiv\underset{\sigma,\sigma^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime\prime},\sigma^{\prime\prime\prime},\sigma^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}\in S_{5}}{\bigcup}\big\{\text{Permutations }\sigma\text{ of all of the player's tensor observables}\big\}\text{. }

Before arguing that the optimal values, bias, and error bounds, of the strong parallel repetition of the
\mathrm{FFL}
game can be characterized with similar arguments presented in previous sections for multiplayer
\mathrm{XOR}
games, we enumerate the possible ways in which observables gathered by each player can be permuted with each other for the
5

\mathrm{XOR}
game, which is denoted with the collection of permutations,

\displaystyle\underline{\mathscr{P}_{5\mathrm{XOR}}}\text{. }

In comparison to error bounds, and the action of associated linear operators provided in
Lemma
1-3
\mathrm{XOR}
, and related results, for the
5

\mathrm{XOR}
, and
N

\mathrm{XOR}
games, alike have structure consisting of error bounds that are far less rigid than those of the two-player
\mathrm{XOR}
and
\mathrm{FFL}
games. In comparison to the equalities,

\displaystyle\bigg(A_{i}\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg)\ket{\psi_{2\mathrm{XOR}}}=\bigg(\textbf{I}\otimes\frac{B_{ij}+B_{ji}}{\sqrt{2}}\bigg)\ket{\psi_{2\mathrm{XOR}}}\text{, }

\displaystyle\bigg(A_{j}\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg)\ket{\psi_{2\mathrm{XOR}}}=\bigg(\textbf{I}\otimes\frac{B_{ij}-B_{ji}}{\sqrt{2}}\bigg)\ket{\psi_{2\mathrm{XOR}}}\text{, }

from the optimal quantum
2

\mathrm{XOR}
strategy, which dictate the transformation that can be applied to Alice’s observable,
A_{i}
, to obtain a superposition of Bob’s observables, or, a transformation that can be applied to superposition of Bob’s observables,
\frac{B_{ij}-B_{ji}}{\sqrt{2}}
, to obtain a superposition of Alice’s observables, there exists a larger combinatorial space of transformations that can be performed on player observables in the
5

\mathrm{XOR}
setting, undoubtedly taking upon more complicated structures.

From the objects defined above, the optimal value of the game satisfies,

\displaystyle\omega_{5\mathrm{XOR}}\big(G\big)\equiv\omega\big({5\mathrm{XOR}}\big)\propto\underset{\ket{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}}},A,B,C,D,E}{\mathrm{sup}}\bra{\psi_{5\mathrm{XOR}}}\underline{\mathscr{P}_{5\mathrm{XOR}}}\ket{\psi_{5\mathrm{XOR}}}\text{.}
