Furthermore, as a generalization of a previous inequality,

\displaystyle\bigg|\bigg|\bigg[\bigg(\big(A_{i}\wedge A_{i^{\prime}}\big)\bigotimes\textbf{I}\bigotimes\bigg(\bigg(\underset{i_{1}\in\mathcal{Q}_{1},i_{2}\in\mathcal{Q}_{2},i_{3}\in\mathcal{Q}_{3}}{\prod}C^{l_{ijk}}_{ijk}\bigg)\wedge\bigg(\underset{i^{\prime}_{1}\in\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{1},i^{\prime}_{2}\in\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{2},i^{\prime}_{3}\in\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{3}}{\prod}C^{l^{\prime}_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}k^{\prime}}}_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}k^{\prime}}\bigg)\bigg)\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-3}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)

\displaystyle-\omega_{\mathrm{XOR}\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathrm{XOR}}\times\bigg(\pm\mathrm{sign}\big(i_{1},j_{1},k_{1},i_{111},\cdots,j_{111},\cdots,k_{111},\cdots,j_{nm(n+m)},\cdots,k_{nm(n+m)}\big)

\displaystyle\times\mathrm{sign}\big(i^{\prime}_{1},j^{\prime}_{1},k^{\prime}_{1},i^{\prime}_{111},\cdots,j^{\prime}_{111},\cdots,k^{\prime}_{111},\cdots,j^{\prime}_{nm(n+m)},\cdots,k^{\prime}_{nm(n+m)}\big)\bigg)\bigg[\textbf{I}\bigotimes\textbf{I}

\displaystyle\bigotimes\bigg(\bigg(\underset{i_{3}\in i\mathcal{Q}_{3},\mathcal{Q}_{3}\oplus 1\equiv\mathcal{Q}_{3}+1}{\underset{i_{2}\in\mathcal{Q}_{2},\mathcal{Q}_{2}\oplus 1\equiv\mathcal{Q}_{2}+1}{\underset{i_{1}\in\mathcal{Q}_{1},\mathcal{Q}_{1}\oplus 1\equiv\mathcal{Q}_{1}+1}{\prod}}}C^{l_{ijk}}_{ijk}\bigg)\wedge\bigg(\underset{i^{\prime}_{3}\in\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{3},\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{3}\oplus 1\equiv\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{3}+1}{\underset{i^{\prime}_{2}\in\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{2},\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{2}\oplus 1\equiv\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{2}+1}{\underset{i^{\prime}_{1}\in\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{1},\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{1}\oplus 1\equiv\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{1}+1}{\prod}}}C^{l^{\prime}_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}k^{\prime}}}_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}k^{\prime}}\bigg)\bigg)\bigotimes\bigg(\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-3}{\prod}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)\bigg]\bigg)\bigg]

\displaystyle\times\ket{\psi_{\mathrm{XOR}\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathrm{XOR}}}\bigg|\bigg|\text{, }

from the fact that the optimal value, under strong parallel repetition, satisfies,

\displaystyle\omega_{\mathrm{XOR}\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathrm{XOR}}\big(G_{\mathrm{XOR}\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathrm{XOR}}\big)\equiv\omega\big(\mathrm{XOR}\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathrm{XOR}\big)\equiv\underset{\#\text{ of strong parallel repetitions }j}{\prod}\omega\big(\mathrm{XOR}\big)^{j}\text{,}

the following summation, with respect to the Frobenius norm, is also expected to have a suitable upper bound,

\displaystyle\bigg|\bigg|\bigg[\bigg(\bigg(\big(A_{1}\big)^{(1)}_{i}\wedge\cdots\wedge\big(A_{n}\big)^{(1)}_{i^{\prime}}\bigg)\bigotimes\textbf{I}\bigotimes\bigg(\bigg(\underset{i_{1}\in\mathcal{Q}_{1},i_{2}\in\mathcal{Q}_{2},i_{3}\in\mathcal{Q}_{3}}{\prod}C^{l_{ijk}}_{ijk}\bigg)\wedge\cdots

\displaystyle\wedge\bigg(\underset{i^{\prime\cdots\prime}_{3}\in\mathcal{Q}^{\prime\cdots\prime}_{3}}{\underset{i^{\prime\cdots\prime}_{1}\in\mathcal{Q}^{\prime\cdots\prime}_{1}}{\underset{i^{\prime\cdots\prime}_{2}\in\mathcal{Q}^{\prime\cdots\prime}_{2}}{\prod}}}C^{l^{\prime\cdots\prime}_{i^{\prime\cdots\prime}j^{\prime\cdots\prime}k^{\prime\cdots\prime}}}_{i^{\prime\cdots\prime}j^{\prime\cdots\prime}k^{\prime\cdots\prime}}\bigg)\bigg)\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-3}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)-\big(\omega\big({\mathrm{XOR}}\big)\big)^{n}

\displaystyle\times\bigg(\pm\mathrm{sign}\big(i_{1},j_{1},k_{1},i_{111},\cdots,j_{111},\cdots,j_{nm(n+m)},k_{111},\cdots,k_{nm(n+m)}\big)

\displaystyle\times\mathrm{sign}\big(i^{\prime}_{1},j^{\prime}_{1},k^{\prime}_{1},i^{\prime}_{111},\cdots,j^{\prime}_{111},\cdots,j^{\prime}_{nm(n+m)},k^{\prime}_{111},\cdots,k^{\prime}_{nm(n+m)}\big)\times\cdots

\displaystyle\times\mathrm{sign}\big(i^{\prime\cdots\prime}_{1},j^{\prime\cdots\prime}_{1},k^{\prime\cdots\prime}_{1},i^{\prime\cdots\prime}_{111},\cdots,j^{\prime\cdots\prime}_{111},\cdots,j^{\prime\cdots\prime}_{nm(n+m)},k^{\prime\cdots\prime}_{111},\cdots,k^{\prime\cdots\prime}_{nm(n+m)}\big)\bigg)

\displaystyle\times\bigg[\textbf{I}\bigotimes\textbf{I}\bigotimes\bigg(\bigg(\underset{i_{1}\in\mathcal{Q}_{1},i_{2}\in\mathcal{Q}_{2},i_{3}\in\mathcal{Q}_{3}}{\prod}C^{l_{ijk}}_{ijk}\bigg)\wedge\cdots\wedge\bigg(\underset{i^{\prime\cdots\prime}_{3}\in\mathcal{Q}^{\prime\cdots\prime}_{3}}{\underset{i^{\prime\cdots\prime}_{1}\in\mathcal{Q}^{\prime\cdots\prime}_{1}}{\underset{i^{\prime\cdots\prime}_{2}\in\mathcal{Q}^{\prime\cdots\prime}_{2}}{\prod}}}C^{l^{\prime\cdots\prime}_{i^{\prime\cdots\prime}j^{\prime\cdots\prime}k^{\prime\cdots\prime}}}_{i^{\prime\cdots\prime}j^{\prime\cdots\prime}k^{\prime\cdots\prime}}\bigg)\bigg)

\displaystyle\bigotimes\bigg(\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq n-3}{\prod}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)\bigg]\bigg)\bigg]\ket{\psi_{\mathrm{XOR}\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathrm{XOR}}}\bigg|\bigg|_{F}\text{, }

(*)

which will be shown to hold later in the section following the introduction of the Bell states in the
N
-player setting. In the two-dimensional case, corresponding to the dynamics between two players, the regularity of structures in the
\mathrm{XOR}
and
\mathrm{FFL}
games is captured with the following collection of four actions,

\displaystyle\bigg(\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg)\bigg(\frac{\ket{00}+\ket{11}}{\sqrt{2}}\bigg)=\frac{\ket{00}+\ket{11}}{\sqrt{2}}\text{ }\text{ , }\bigg(\sigma_{x}\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg)\bigg(\frac{\ket{00}+\ket{11}}{\sqrt{2}}\bigg)=\frac{\ket{10}+\ket{01}}{\sqrt{2}}\text{, }

\displaystyle\bigg(\sigma_{z}\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg)\bigg(\frac{\ket{00}+\ket{11}}{\sqrt{2}}\bigg)=\frac{\ket{00}-\ket{11}}{\sqrt{2}}\text{ }\text{ , }\bigg(\sigma_{x}\sigma_{z}\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg)\bigg(\frac{\ket{00}+\ket{11}}{\sqrt{2}}\bigg)=\frac{\ket{10}-\ket{01}}{\sqrt{2}}\text{. }

The Bell states for games with more players, as a special case of the generalization provided for the
N
-player
\mathrm{XOR}
game in the appendix, take the form,

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\overset{N-3}{\cdots}\otimes\textbf{I}}{\sqrt{N}}\bigg)\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq N}{\sum}\ket{\text{Player }j\text{ state}}\bigg)\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq N}{\sum}\ket{\text{Player }j\text{ state}}\bigg)\text{, }

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{\sigma_{z}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\overset{N-3}{\cdots}\otimes\textbf{I}}{\sqrt{N}}\bigg)\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq N}{\sum}\ket{\text{Player }j\text{ state}}\bigg)\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\bigg(\ket{\text{Player 1 state}}

\displaystyle-\ket{\text{Player 2 state}}+\ket{\text{Player 3 state}}+\cdots+\ket{\text{Player }N\text{ state}}\bigg)\text{, }

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{\textbf{I}\otimes\sigma_{z}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\overset{N-3}{\cdots}\otimes\textbf{I}}{\sqrt{N}}\bigg)\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq N}{\sum}\ket{\text{Player }j\text{ state}}\bigg)\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq 2}{\sum}\ket{\text{Player }j\text{ state}}

\displaystyle-\ket{\text{Player }3\text{ state}}+\underset{4\leq j\leq N}{\sum}\ket{\text{Player }j\text{ state}}\bigg)\text{, }

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\overset{N-4}{\cdots}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\sigma_{z}}{\sqrt{N}}\bigg)\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq N}{\sum}\ket{\text{Player }j\text{ state}}\bigg)\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\bigg(-\ket{\text{Player }1\text{ state}}

\displaystyle+\underset{2\leq j\leq N}{\sum}\ket{\text{Player }j\text{ state}}\bigg)\text{, }

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{\sigma_{x}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\overset{N-2}{\cdots}\otimes\textbf{I}}{\sqrt{N}}\bigg)\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq N}{\sum}\ket{\text{Player }j\text{ state}}\bigg)\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\bigg(\widetilde{\ket{\text{Player }1\text{ state}}}

\displaystyle+\widetilde{\ket{\text{Player }2\text{ state}}}+\underset{3\leq j\leq N}{\sum}\ket{\text{Player }j\text{ state}}\bigg)\text{, }

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{\textbf{I}\otimes\sigma_{x}\otimes\overset{N-2}{\cdots}\otimes\textbf{I}}{\sqrt{N}}\bigg)\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq N}{\sum}\ket{\text{Player }j\text{ state}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\bigg(\ket{\text{Player }1\text{ state}}+\widetilde{\ket{\text{Player }2\text{ state}}}+\widetilde{\ket{\text{Player }3\text{ state}}}

\displaystyle+\underset{4\leq j\leq N}{\sum}\ket{\text{Player }j\text{ state}}\bigg)\text{, }

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\overset{N-4}{\cdots}\otimes\sigma_{x}\otimes\textbf{I}}{\sqrt{N}}\bigg)\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq N}{\sum}\ket{\text{Player }j\text{ state}}\bigg)\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq N-2}{\sum}\ket{\text{Player }j\text{ state}}

\displaystyle+\widetilde{\ket{\text{Player }(N-1)\text{ state}}}+\widetilde{\ket{\text{Player }N\text{ state}}}\bigg)\text{, }

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\overset{N-4}{\cdots}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\sigma_{x}}{\sqrt{N}}\bigg)\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq N}{\sum}\ket{\text{Player }j\text{ state}}\bigg)\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\bigg(\widetilde{\ket{\text{Player }1\text{ state}}}

\displaystyle+\underset{2\leq j\leq N-1}{\sum}\ket{\text{Player }j\text{ state}}+\widetilde{\ket{\text{Player }N\text{ state}}}\bigg)\text{, }

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{\sigma_{z}\sigma_{x}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\overset{N-2}{\cdots}\otimes\textbf{I}}{\sqrt{N}}\bigg)\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq N}{\sum}\ket{\text{Player }j\text{ state}}\bigg)\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\bigg(\widetilde{\ket{\text{Player }1\text{ state}}}

\displaystyle+\widetilde{\ket{\text{Player }2\text{ state}}}-\underset{3\leq j\leq N}{\sum}\ket{\text{Player }j\text{ state}}\bigg)\text{,}

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{\textbf{I}\otimes\sigma_{z}\sigma_{x}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\overset{N-3}{\cdots}\otimes\textbf{I}}{\sqrt{N}}\bigg)\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq N}{\sum}\ket{\text{Player }j\text{ state}}\bigg)\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\bigg(-\ket{\text{Player }1\text{ state}}

\displaystyle+\widetilde{\ket{\text{Player }2\text{ state}}}+\widetilde{\ket{\text{Player }3\text{ state}}}-\underset{4\leq j\leq N}{\sum}\ket{\text{Player }j\text{ state}}\bigg)\text{, }

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\overset{N-4}{\cdots}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\sigma_{z}\sigma_{x}}{\sqrt{N}}\bigg)\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq N}{\sum}\ket{\text{Player }j\text{ state}}\bigg)\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\bigg(\widetilde{\ket{\text{Player }1\text{ state}}}

\displaystyle+\underset{2\leq j\leq N-2}{\sum}\ket{\text{Player }j\text{ state}}-\ket{\text{Player }(N-1)\text{ state}}+\widetilde{\ket{\text{Player }N\text{ state}}}\bigg)\text{, }

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{\sigma_{x}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\overset{N-4}{\cdots}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\sigma_{z}}{\sqrt{N}}\bigg)\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq N}{\sum}\ket{\text{Player }j\text{ state}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\bigg(-\widetilde{\ket{\text{Player }1\text{ state}}}+\widetilde{\ket{\text{Player }2\text{ state}}}

\displaystyle-\underset{3\leq j\leq N}{\sum}\ket{\text{Player }j\text{ state}}\bigg)\text{, }

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{\textbf{I}\otimes\sigma_{x}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\overset{N-5}{\cdots}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\sigma_{z}}{\sqrt{N}}\bigg)\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq N}{\sum}\ket{\text{Player }j\text{ state}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\bigg(-\ket{\text{Player }1\text{ state}}+\widetilde{\ket{\text{Player }2\text{ state}}}

\displaystyle+\widetilde{\ket{\text{Player }3\text{ state}}}+\underset{4\leq j\leq N-1}{\sum}\ket{\text{Player }j\text{ state}}-\ket{\text{Player }N\text{ state}}\bigg)\text{, }

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{\sigma_{z}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\overset{N-4}{\cdots}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\sigma_{x}}{\sqrt{N}}\bigg)\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq N}{\sum}\ket{\text{Player }j\text{ state}}\bigg)\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\bigg(-\widetilde{\ket{\text{Player }1\text{ state}}}

\displaystyle+\underset{2\leq j\leq N-1}{\sum}\ket{\text{Player }j\text{ state}}-\widetilde{\ket{\text{Player }N\text{ state}}}\bigg)\text{, }

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{\textbf{I}\otimes\sigma_{z}\otimes\overset{N-4}{\cdots}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\sigma_{x}}{\sqrt{N}}\bigg)\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq N}{\sum}\ket{\text{Player }j\text{ state}}\bigg)\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\bigg(\widetilde{\ket{\text{Player }1\text{ state}}}

\displaystyle+\ket{\text{Player }2\text{ state}}-\ket{\text{Player }3\text{ state}}+\underset{4\leq j\leq N-1}{\sum}\ket{\text{Player }j\text{ state}}+\widetilde{\ket{\text{Player }N\text{ state}}}\bigg)\text{, }

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\overset{N-4}{\cdots}\otimes\sigma_{z}\otimes\sigma_{x}}{\sqrt{N}}\bigg)\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq N}{\sum}\ket{\text{Player }j\text{ state}}\bigg)\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\bigg(\widetilde{\ket{\text{Player }1\text{ state}}}

\displaystyle+\ket{\text{Player }2\text{ state}}\underset{3\leq j\leq N-1}{\sum}\ket{\text{Player }j\text{ state}}-\widetilde{\ket{\text{Player }N\text{ state}}}\bigg)\text{. }

In the set of relations above for the Bell states, the position at which the
\sigma_{x}
is applied to the summation over all player states,

\displaystyle\underset{1\leq j\leq n}{\sum}\ket{\text{Player }j\text{ state}}\text{, }

in addition to
\sigma_{z}
is applied. Furthermore,
\sigma_{x}
and
\sigma_{z}
can be simultaneously applied to tensor products, which can result in the transformation of the player observable,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\ket{\text{Player }1\text{ state}}}\equiv\text{1 st player tensor observable}\big(i^{\prime}_{1},i_{2}\big)\text{, }

\displaystyle\widetilde{\ket{\text{Player }2\text{ state}}}\equiv\text{2 nd player tensor observable}\big(i_{1},i^{\prime}_{2}\big)\text{, }

with the
\widetilde{\cdot}
transformation being similarly defined for all other observables for the remaining players of the game. From the Bell states introduced above, by adapting an argument from a previous subsection, , the expression introduced in
(*)
acting on,

\displaystyle\ket{\psi_{\mathrm{XOR}\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathrm{XOR}}}\equiv\underset{\#\text{ of strong parallel repetitions}}{\bigwedge}\ket{\psi_{\mathrm{XOR}}}\equiv\ket{\psi^{\underset{\#\text{ of strong parallel repetitions}}{\wedge}}_{\mathrm{XOR}}}\text{, }

can be upper bounded with the following argument to show that the following result holds:

Lemma

FR

\wedge\cdots\wedge

FR
(
Frobenius norm upper bound for strong parallel XOR repetition
). One has that,

(*)\lesssim N!n^{N}\sqrt{\epsilon_{\mathrm{XOR}\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathrm{XOR}}}\times\left\{\!\begin{array}[]{ll@{}>{{}}l}n^{\frac{\#\text{ of players}}{2}+5}\Longleftrightarrow\big(\#\text{ of players}\big)\mathrm{mod}2\equiv 0\\
n^{\lfloor\frac{\#\text{ of players}}{2}\rfloor+5}\Longleftrightarrow\big(\#\text{ of players}\big)\mathrm{mod}2\neq 0\end{array}\right.\text{, }

given
\epsilon_{\mathrm{XOR}\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathrm{XOR}}
sufficiently small.

Proof of Lemma FR
\wedge\cdots\wedge
FR
. We present an adaptation of an argument for the
N

\mathrm{XOR}
game which is included in the next section following arguments for demonstrating that a certain suitably defined tensor is positive semidefinite. Namely, for upper bounding the Frobenius norm for
\mathrm{XOR}
strong parallel repetition, fix
\epsilon\equiv\epsilon^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{XOR}\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathrm{XOR}}
sufficiently small (not to be confused with the series of constants
\epsilon
,
\epsilon^{\prime}
,
\epsilon^{\prime\prime}
, and
\epsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}
introduced for upper bounding the Frobenius norm in the next section), from which the assumptions for the
\mathrm{XOR}
strong parallel repetition game,

\displaystyle\big(N\mathrm{XOR}\big)\wedge\cdots\wedge\big(N\mathrm{XOR}\big)\equiv\big(\mathrm{XOR}\big)\wedge\cdots\wedge\big(\mathrm{XOR}\big)\equiv\underset{\#\text{ of strong parallel repetitions }j}{\bigwedge}\big(\mathrm{XOR}\big)^{j}

\displaystyle\equiv\underset{1\leq j\leq n}{\bigwedge}\big(\mathrm{XOR}\big)^{j}\text{,}

include,

\displaystyle\bigg[\bigg[\textbf{I}-\textbf{I}\bigg(\frac{\pm\big(A_{k}\wedge\cdots\wedge A_{k^{\prime\cdots\prime}}\big)+\big(A_{l}\wedge\cdots\wedge A_{l^{\prime\cdots\prime}}\big)}{\sqrt{2}}\bigg)\bigg]\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\overset{N-5}{\cdots}\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg]

\displaystyle\times\ket{\psi_{\mathrm{XOR}\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathrm{XOR}}}<\sqrt{N!n\big(n-1\big)\big(n-2\big)\big(n-3\big)\big(n-4\big)}\epsilon\text{, }

\displaystyle\bigg[\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\overset{N-5}{\cdots}\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg]\ket{\psi_{\mathrm{XOR}\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathrm{XOR}}}<\sqrt{n\big(n-1\big)\big(n-2\big)}\epsilon\text{, }

\displaystyle\bigg[\bigg[\textbf{I}-\big(\omega_{\mathrm{XOR}}\big)^{n}\bigg(\mathrm{sign}\big(i_{1},\cdots,i_{n},j_{1},\cdots,j_{n},k_{1},\cdots,k_{n}\big)\times\cdots\times\mathrm{sign}\big(i^{\prime\cdots\prime}_{1}

\displaystyle,\cdots,i^{\prime\cdots\prime}_{n},j^{\prime\cdots\prime}_{1},\cdots,j^{\prime\cdots\prime}_{n},k^{\prime\cdots\prime}_{1},\cdots,k^{\prime\cdots\prime}_{n}\big)\bigg)\textbf{I}\bigg]\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\overset{N-5}{\cdots}\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg]

\displaystyle\times\ket{\psi_{\mathrm{XOR}\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathrm{XOR}}}<n^{N}\sqrt{\epsilon}\text{. }

Hence, from the three assumptions on the strong parallel repetition game above, the desired upper bound dependent upon the number of players in the game takes the form,

\displaystyle\sqrt{N!n\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq\#\text{ of players}-1}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)\epsilon+100\omega_{N\mathrm{XOR}}n^{\#\text{ of players}}\epsilon}

\displaystyle<\sqrt{N!n\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq\#\text{ of players}-1}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)\epsilon+1000n^{\#\text{ of players}}\epsilon}\text{, }

which can be further rearranged as,

\displaystyle\sqrt{\bigg(N!n\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq\#\text{ of players}-1}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)+1000n^{\#\text{ of players}}\bigg)\epsilon}

\displaystyle<\sqrt{\bigg(N!n\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq\#\text{ of players}-1}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)+1000n^{\#\text{ of players}}\bigg)}\big(10\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)

\displaystyle<\sqrt{2N!n\big(n-\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)\big)\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq\#\text{ of players}-2}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)+1000n^{\#\text{ of players}}}

\displaystyle\times\big(10\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)

\displaystyle\equiv\sqrt{\big(2N!n^{2}-n\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)\big)\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq\#\text{ of players}-2}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)+1000n^{\#\text{ of players}}}

\displaystyle\times\big(10\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)

\displaystyle<\sqrt{20N!n^{2}\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq\#\text{ of players}-2}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)-n\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq\#\text{ of players}-2}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{+1000n^{\#\text{ of players}}}

\displaystyle\times\big(10\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)

\displaystyle<\sqrt{20N!n^{2}\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq\#\text{ of players}-2}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)-n\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)\big(n-\big(\#\text{ of players}-2\big)\big)\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{\times\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq\#\text{ of players}-3}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)+1000n^{\#\text{ of players}}}\big(11\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)\text{. }

As the product over the number of players is taken, the final term above can be upper bounded with,

\displaystyle\sqrt{20N!n^{2}\big(n-\big(\#\text{ of players}-2\big)\big)\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq\#\text{ of players}-3}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)-\bigg(n^{2}\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)+\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)\big(\#\text{ of players}-2\big)\bigg)\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq\#\text{ of players}-3}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)+1000n^{\#\text{ of players}}}\big(11\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)

\displaystyle<\sqrt{20N!n^{2}\big(n\big(\#\text{ of players}-2\big)\big)\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq\#\text{ of players}-3}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)-\frac{1}{2}\bigg(n^{2}\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{+\big(\#\text{of players}-1\big)\big(\#\text{of players}-2\big)\bigg)\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq\#\text{of players}-3}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)+1000n^{\#\text{ of players}}}\big(11\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)

\displaystyle\equiv\sqrt{20N!n^{2}\big(n-\big(\#\text{of players}-2\big)\big)\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq\#\text{of players}-3}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)-\frac{1}{2}\bigg(n^{2}\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{+\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)\big(\#\text{ of players}-2\big)\bigg)\big(n-\big(\#\text{ of players}-3\big)\big)\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq\#\text{ of players}-4}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{+1000n^{\#\text{ of players}}}\big(11\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)\text{. }

(*)

Proceeding, the expression above, denoted with
(*)
, equals,

\displaystyle(\textit{*})\equiv\sqrt{20N!n^{2}\big(n-\big(\#\text{ of players}-2\big)\big)\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq\#\text{ of players}-3}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)-\frac{1}{2}\bigg(-n^{2}\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{\times\big(\#\text{ of players}-3\big)\bigg)+n^{3}\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)+n\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)\big(\#\text{ of players }-2\big)\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{-\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)\big(\#\text{ of players }-2\big)\big(\#\text{ of players}-3\big)\bigg)\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq\#\text{ of players}-5}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{+1000n^{\#\text{ of players }}}\big(11\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)

\displaystyle<\sqrt{20N!n^{2}\big(n-\big(\#\text{ of players}-2\big)\big)\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq\#\text{ of players}-3}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{-\frac{1}{2}\bigg(n^{2}\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)\big(\#\text{ of players}-3\big)+n^{3}\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)+n\big(\#\text{ of players}\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{-1\big)\big(\#\text{ of players}-2\big)-\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)\big(\#\text{ of players}-2\big)\big(\#\text{ of players}-3\big)\bigg)\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{\times\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq\#\text{ of players}}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)+1000n^{\#\text{ of players}}}\big(11\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)

\displaystyle<\sqrt{20N!n^{2}\big(n-\big(\#\text{ of players}-2\big)\big)\big(n-\#\text{ of players}-3\big)\bigg)\big(n-1\big)-\frac{1}{2}\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{\times\bigg(-n^{2}\big(\#\text{of players }-1\big)\big(\#\text{ of players}-3\big)\big(n-\big(\#\text{of players}-4\big)\big)+n^{3}\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{\times\big(n-\big(\#\text{ of players}-4\big)\big)+n\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)\big(\#\text{ of players}-2\big)\big(n-\big(\#\text{ of players}-4\big)\big)\bigg)\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{\times\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq\#\text{ of players}-5}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)+1000n^{\#\text{ of players}}}\big(11\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)

\displaystyle<\sqrt{20N!n^{2}\big(n-\big(\#\text{ of players}-2\big)\big)\big(n-\big(\#\text{ of players}-3\big)\big)\times\cdots\times\big(n-1\big)-\frac{1}{4}\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{\times\big(-n^{2}\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)\big(\#\text{ of players}-3\big)\big(\#\text{ of players}-4\big)\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{+n^{3}\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)\big(\#\text{ of players}-4\big)+n\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)\big(\#\text{ of players}-2\big)\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{\times\big(\#\text{ of players}-4\big)\big)\bigg)\bigg(n^{\#\text{ of players - 5}}\bigg)+1000n^{\#\text{ of players}}}\big(11\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)\text{. }

(**)

Proceeding,

\displaystyle(\textit{**})<\sqrt{20N!n^{2}\bigg(n-\big(\#\text{ of players}-2\big)\big)\big(n-\big(\#\text{ of players}-3\big)\big)\times\cdots\times\big(n-1\big)-\frac{1}{4}\big(-n^{2}\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{\times\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)\big(\#\text{ of players}-3\big)\big(\#\text{ of players}-4\big)+n^{3}\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{\times\big(\#\text{ of players}-4\big)+n\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)\big(\#\text{ of players}-2\big)\big(\#\text{ of players}-4\big)\bigg)\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{\times\bigg(n^{\#\text{ of players}-5}\bigg)+1000n^{\#\text{ of players}}}\big(11\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)

\displaystyle\equiv\sqrt{20N!n^{2}\bigg(n-\big(\#\text{ of players}-2\big)\big(n-\big(\#\text{ of players}-3\big)\big)\times\cdots\times\big(n-1\big)-\frac{1}{4}\big(-n^{2}\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{\times\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)\big(\#\text{ of players}-3\big)\big(\#\text{ of players}-4\big)+n\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{\times\big(\#\text{ of players}-2\big)\big(\#\text{ of players}-4\big)\bigg)\bigg(n^{\#\text{ of players}-5}\bigg)+1000n^{\#\text{ of players}}}\big(11\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)

\displaystyle\equiv\sqrt{\bigg(20N!n^{3}-100n^{2}\big(\#\text{ of players}-2\big)\bigg(\big(n-\big(\#\text{ of players}-3\big)\big)\times\cdots\times\big(n-1\big)\bigg)\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{-\frac{1}{4}\bigg(-n^{2}\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)\big(\#\text{ of players}-3\big)\big(\#\text{ of players}-4\big)+n^{3}\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{\times\big(\#\text{ of players}-4\big)n^{\#\text{of players}-5}+n\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)\big(\#\text{ of players}-2\big)\bigg)\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{\times\big(\#\text{ of players}-4\big)n^{\#\text{ of players}-5}\bigg)+1000n^{\#\text{ of players}}}\big(11\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)\text{. }

(***)

The final expression above can be further rearranged to obtain the desired upper bound, from the observations that,

\displaystyle(\textit{***})<\sqrt{\bigg(20N!n^{3}-50n^{2}\big(n-2\big)\bigg)\big(n-1\big)^{\#\text{ of players}-5}-\frac{1}{4}\bigg(-n^{2}\big(\#\text{ of players - 1}\big)^{3}\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{\times n^{\#\text{ of players}-5}+n^{3}\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)^{2}n^{\#\text{ of players}-5}+n\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)^{3}n^{\#\text{ of players}-5}\bigg)\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{+1000n^{\#\text{ of players}}}\big(11\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)

\displaystyle\equiv\sqrt{\bigg(20N!n^{3}-50n^{2}\big(n-2\big)\big)\big(n-1\big)^{\#\text{ of players}-3}\bigg)-\frac{1}{4}\big(\big(\#\text{ of players}\big)-1\big)^{2}n\bigg(-n\big(\#\text{ of players}\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{-1\big)n^{\#\text{ of players}-5}+n^{\big(\#\text{ of players}-5\big)+1}+\big(\#\text{ of players}n^{\#\text{ of players}-5}\bigg)+1000n^{\#\text{ of players}}}

\displaystyle\times\big(11\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)

\displaystyle<\sqrt{\bigg(20N!n^{\#\text{ of players}}-50n^{\#\text{ of players}}-\bigg)-\frac{1}{4}n^{3}\bigg(-n\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)n^{\#\text{ of players}-5}\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{+n^{\#\text{ of players}-5}+\big(\#\text{ of players }-5\big)n^{\#\text{ of players}-5}\bigg)+1000n^{\#\text{of players}-5}}\big(11\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)

\displaystyle<\sqrt{n^{\#\text{ of players}}\bigg(n^{\#\text{ of players}}-n^{3}\bigg(n^{\#\text{ of players}-4}+2n^{\#\text{ of players}-5}\bigg)\bigg)+1000n^{\#\text{of players}-5}}\big(110\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)

\displaystyle<\sqrt{\bigg(n^{\#\text{ of players}}-n^{3}\bigg(-n^{\#\text{ of players}-5}+2n^{\#\text{ of players}-5}\bigg)+1000n^{\#\text{ of players}}\bigg)}\big(120\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)\text{.}

(****)

The final desired upper bound,

\mathcal{I}\equiv\big(130N!n^{N}\big)\sqrt{\epsilon}\times\left\{\!\begin{array}[]{ll@{}>{{}}l}n^{\frac{\#\text{ of players}}{2}+5}\Longleftrightarrow\big(\#\text{ of players}\big)\mathrm{mod}2\equiv 0\\
n^{\lfloor\frac{\#\text{ of players}}{2}\rfloor+5}\Longleftrightarrow\big(\#\text{ of players}\big)\mathrm{mod}2\neq 0\end{array}\right.\text{, }

for the N
\mathrm{XOR}
game above is obtained from the observations that,

\displaystyle(\textit{****})<\sqrt{N!n^{\#\text{ of players}-3}\bigg(1+n^{6}-2n^{5}+1000\bigg)}\big(120\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)<\sqrt{N!n^{\#\text{ of players}-3}\bigg(1+n^{6}+998n^{5}\bigg)}

\displaystyle\times\big(120\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)

\displaystyle<\sqrt{N!n^{\#\text{ of players}-3}\bigg(1+999n^{6}\bigg)}\big(120\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)<\sqrt{N!n^{\#\text{ of players}}n^{8}}\big(120N!n^{N}\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)<\mathcal{I}\text{,}

from which we conclude the argument.

2.7
Positive semidefinite tensors

For two
\pm
observables,
A
and
B
, in [37], the operator,

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{A+B}{\sqrt{2}}+\frac{A+B}{\big|A+B\big|}\bigg)^{2}\equiv\bigg(\big(A+B\big)\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}+\frac{1}{\big|A+B\big|}\bigg)\bigg)^{2}\text{, }

is characterized as being positive semidefinite, with eigenvalues,

\displaystyle\bigg[\mathrm{sign}\big(\lambda\big)\frac{\lambda^{2}}{2}-1\bigg]^{2}\text{. }

For additional
\pm
observables corresponding to the results of
N
players, the counterpart to the two-player expression for the operator above takes the form,

\displaystyle\bigg[\frac{\sum\text{Tensors of player observables}}{\sqrt{N}}+\frac{\sum\text{Tensors of player observables}}{\big|\sum\text{Tensors of player observables}\big|}\bigg]^{N}\text{, }

where the summation is taken over all possible responses of each player to a question drawn from the referee’s probability distribution, namely,

\displaystyle\sum\text{Tensors of player observables}\equiv\underset{\text{Questions}}{\sum}\text{Tensors of player observables}\text{, }

which will be shown to have the eigenvalues that are proportional to,

\displaystyle\bigg[\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\bigg[\underset{1\leq j\leq N-1}{\sum}\big(\mathrm{sign}\big(\lambda\big)\big)^{j}\lambda^{N-j}\bigg]-1\bigg]^{N}\text{. }

As introduced in previous sections from optimal values, linear operators, and error bounds, from the multiplayer setting, under the strong parallel repetition operation, for
\mathrm{XOR}
and
\mathrm{FFL}
, games, denote,

\displaystyle\underset{\text{Questions}\wedge\cdots\wedge\text{Questions}}{\sum}\big(\big(\text{Tensors of XOR player observables}\big)\wedge\cdots\wedge\big(\text{Tensors of XOR}

\displaystyle\text{ player observables}\big)\big)

\displaystyle\equiv{\sum^{\prime}}\big(\big(\text{Tensors of XOR player observables}\big)\wedge\cdots\wedge\big(\text{Tensors of XOR player observables}\big)\big)\text{, }

\displaystyle\underset{\text{Questions}\wedge\text{Questions}}{\sum}\big(\big(\text{Tensors of FFL player observables}\big)\wedge\cdots\wedge\big(\text{Tensors of FFL}

\displaystyle\text{ player observables}\big)\big)

\displaystyle\equiv{\sum^{\prime\prime}}\big(\big(\text{Tensors of FFL player observables}\big)\wedge\cdots\wedge\big(\text{Tensors of FFL}

\displaystyle\text{ player observables}\big)\big)\text{, }

from the question sets obtained under an arbitrary, or under two, applications of strong parallel repetition, from which one expects that the operators should take the forms,

\displaystyle\bigg[\frac{\sum^{\prime}\big(\big(\text{Tensors of XOR player observables}\big)\wedge\cdots\wedge\big(\text{Tensors of XOR player observables}\big)\big)}{\sqrt{N}}

\displaystyle+\frac{\sum^{\prime}\big(\big(\text{Tensors of XOR player observables}\big)\wedge\cdots\wedge\big(\text{Tensors of XOR player observables}\big)\big)}{\big|\sum^{\prime}\big(\big(\text{Tensors of XOR player observables}\big)\wedge\cdots\wedge\big(\text{Tensors of XOR player observables}\big)\big)\big|}\bigg]^{N}\text{, }

\displaystyle\bigg[\frac{\sum^{\prime\prime}\big(\big(\text{Tensors of FFL player observables}\big)\wedge\big(\text{Tensors of FFL player observables}\big)\big)}{\sqrt{N}}

\displaystyle+\frac{\sum^{\prime\prime}\big(\big(\text{Tensors of FFL player observables}\big)\wedge\big(\text{Tensors of FFL player observables}\big)\big)}{\big|\sum^{\prime\prime}\big(\big(\text{Tensors of FFL player observables}\big)\wedge\big(\text{Tensors of FFL player observables}\big)\big)\big|}\bigg]^{N}\text{, }

respectively.

Lemma
(
N-XOR positive semidefinite tensors
). The superposition of
N

\pm 1
observables,

\displaystyle\bigg[\frac{\sum\text{Tensors of player observables}}{\sqrt{N}}+\frac{\sum\text{Tensors of player observables}}{\big|\sum\text{Tensors of player observables}\big|}\bigg]^{N}\text{, }

is positive semidefinite, which can be concluded from the fact that,

\displaystyle\bigg[N+\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\bigg)^{2}\bigg(\underset{1\leq i\leq N-1}{\sum}n^{i}\mathrm{sign}\big(\lambda\big)\lambda^{N-i}\bigg)^{2}-2\bigg(\underset{1\leq i\leq N-1}{\sum}n^{i}\mathrm{sign}\big(\lambda\big)\lambda^{N-i}\bigg)\bigg]\bigg[\bigg[\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}

\displaystyle\times\bigg[\underset{1\leq j\leq N-1}{\sum}\big(\mathrm{sign}\big(\lambda\big)\big)^{j}\lambda^{N-j}\bigg]-1\bigg]^{N}\bigg]^{-1}\bigg[N+\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\bigg)^{2}\bigg(\underset{1\leq i\leq N-1}{\sum}n^{i}\mathrm{sign}\big(\lambda\big)\lambda^{N-i}\bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle-2\bigg(\underset{1\leq i\leq N-1}{\sum}n^{i}\mathrm{sign}\big(\lambda\big)\lambda^{N-i}\bigg)\bigg]\text{, }

implies that the
\pm 1
superposition has eigenvalues,

\displaystyle\bigg[\frac{\lambda^{N}-\underset{1\leq i\leq N-1}{\sum}n^{i}\lambda^{N-i}-n}{n}\bigg]^{N}\text{. }

From the closed-form representation of the eigenvalues of the previous operator, one can also conclude, straightforwardly, that the operator,

\displaystyle\bigg[\frac{\sum\text{Tensors of player observables}}{\sqrt{N}}+\frac{\sum\text{Tensors of player observables}}{\big|\sum\text{Tensors of player observables}\big|}\bigg]^{N}

\displaystyle\times\bigg[N+\underset{1\leq i\leq N-1}{\sum}n^{i}\lambda^{N-i}

\displaystyle+N\sqrt{\textbf{I}+\frac{\big(\sum\text{Tensors of player observables}\big)\big(\sum^{-1}\text{Tensors of player observables}\big)}{N}}\bigg]^{-N}

\displaystyle\times\bigg[\frac{\sum\text{Tensors of player observables}}{\sqrt{N}}+\frac{\sum\text{Tensors of player observables}}{\big|\sum\text{Tensors of player observables}\big|}\bigg]^{N}\text{, }

has eigenvalues,

\displaystyle\bigg[\frac{\lambda^{N}-\underset{1\leq i\leq N-1}{\sum}n^{i}\lambda^{N-i}-n}{n}\bigg]^{N}\frac{1}{N+\underset{1\leq i\leq N-1}{\sum}n^{i}\lambda^{N-i}+N\sqrt{1+\frac{\underset{1\leq i\leq N-1}{\sum}n^{i}\lambda^{N-i}-N}{N}}}\text{. }

To show that the closed form expression given above holds for the eigenvalues of the operator from tensor observables of each player, one makes use of a suitable factorization of the expression for the eigenvalues above, which is dependent upon,

\displaystyle N\bigg[\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\bigg[\underset{1\leq j\leq N-1}{\sum}\big(\mathrm{sign}\big(\lambda\big)\big)^{j}\lambda^{N-j}\bigg]-1\bigg]^{N}\text{, }

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\bigg)^{2}\bigg[\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\bigg[\underset{1\leq j\leq N-1}{\sum}\big(\mathrm{sign}\big(\lambda\big)\big)^{j}\lambda^{N-j}\bigg]-1\bigg]^{N}\text{, }

\displaystyle-2\bigg(\underset{1\leq i\leq N-1}{\sum}n^{i}\mathrm{sign}\big(\lambda\big)\lambda^{N-i}\bigg)\bigg[\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\bigg[\underset{1\leq j\leq N-1}{\sum}\big(\mathrm{sign}\big(\lambda\big)\big)^{j}\lambda^{N-j}\bigg]-1\bigg]^{N}\text{. }

Proof of Lemma
. The result is a direct application of
Lemma
from [37], which is included in
2.5
- the fifth section of the Appendix.

2.8
Player dependent upper bounds at optimality

We argue that the desired upper bounds hold, up to constants, of order
n^{3}\sqrt{\epsilon}
,
n^{4}\sqrt{\epsilon}
, and
n^{5}\sqrt{\epsilon}
, respectively. In the most simple case, for the 3-
\mathrm{XOR}
game, an upper bound for,

\displaystyle\bigg|\bigg|\bigg[\bigg(\bigg(\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}A^{j_{i}}_{i}\bigg)\otimes B_{kl}\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg)-\omega_{3\mathrm{XOR}}\bigg(\pm\bigg(\mathrm{sign}\big(i_{1},j_{1},\cdots,j_{n}\big)

\displaystyle\times\bigg[\bigg(\bigg(\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}A^{j_{i}}_{i}\bigg)+\bigg(\underset{\text{set }i+1\equiv i\oplus 1}{\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}}A^{j_{i}}_{i}\bigg)\bigg)\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg]\bigg)\bigg)\bigg]\ket{\psi_{3\mathrm{XOR}}}\bigg|\bigg|^{2}\text{.}

For the set of possible answers that the first player can provide in response to a question drawn from the referee’s probability distribution, a variant of the inequality above takes the form,

\displaystyle\bigg|\bigg|\bigg[\bigg(\bigg(\bigg(\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}A^{j_{i}}_{i}\bigg)-\bigg(\bigg(\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}A^{j_{i}}_{i}\bigg)\bigg(\frac{\pm A_{k}+A_{l}}{\sqrt{2}}\bigg)\bigg)\bigg)\otimes B_{kl}\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg)\ket{\psi_{3\mathrm{XOR}}}\bigg|\bigg|

\displaystyle-\omega_{3\mathrm{XOR}}\bigg|\bigg|\bigg[\bigg(\bigg(\bigg(\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}A^{j_{i}}_{i}\bigg)A_{k}-\mathrm{sign}\big(i_{1},j_{1},\cdots,j_{n}\big)\bigg(\underset{\text{set }i+1\equiv i\oplus 1}{\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}}A^{j_{i}}_{i}\bigg)\bigg)\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg)\bigg]

\displaystyle\times\ket{\psi_{3\mathrm{XOR}}}\bigg|\bigg|+\bigg|\bigg|\bigg[\bigg(\bigg(\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}A^{j_{i}}_{i}\bigg)A_{k}-\mathrm{sign}\big(i_{1},j_{1},\cdots,j_{n}\big)\bigg(\underset{\text{set }i+1\equiv i\oplus 1}{\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}}A^{j_{i}}_{i}\bigg)\bigg)\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg]

\displaystyle\times\ket{\psi_{3\mathrm{XOR}}}\bigg|\bigg|\text{. }

implies that the upper bound, up to constant, of order
n^{3}\sqrt{\epsilon}
holds. After obtaining the first desired upper bound, we demonstrate how analagous upper bounds can be obtained for several variants of the
\mathrm{XOR}
game that have been considered in previous sections. In the result below, we exhibit how a computation used for strong parallel repetition of the
\mathrm{XOR}
game can straightforwardly also be applied for
3\mathrm{XOR}
games, and beyond. As such, as a generalization of a computation provided previously by the author, we seek to elucidate properties of the optimal solution state for inequalities that are counterparts to,

\displaystyle\bigg|\bigg|\bigg(\bigg(\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}A^{j_{i}}_{i}\bigg)\otimes B_{kl}\bigg)\ket{\psi_{\mathrm{FFL}}}-\frac{2}{3}\bigg[\pm\bigg(\mathrm{sign}\big(i,j_{1},\cdots,j_{n}\big)\bigg[\text{ }\bigg(\underset{i=j_{k}+1,\text{ }\mathrm{set}\text{ }j_{k}+1\equiv j_{k}\oplus 1}{\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}}A^{j_{i}}_{i}\bigg)

\displaystyle+\bigg(\underset{i=j_{l}+1,\text{ }\mathrm{set}\text{ }j_{l}+1\equiv j_{l}\oplus 1}{\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}}A^{j_{i}}_{i}\bigg)\text{ }\bigg]\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg)\ket{\psi_{\mathrm{FFL}}}\bigg]\bigg|\bigg|<\bigg(\frac{8200\sqrt{2}}{27}\bigg)n^{2}\sqrt{\epsilon}\text{, }

the second
\mathrm{FFL}
error bound.

Lemma

Gen-FFL-Bound
(
generalizations of the second FFL error bound
,
6.6
, [37],
Lemma

7
, [44]). Denote the quantum states corresponding to optimal strategies,

\displaystyle\ket{\psi_{3\mathrm{XOR}}}\equiv\underset{\text{Players}}{\bigcup}\underset{\mathcal{S}}{\mathrm{sup}}\big\{\text{A player's quantum strategy }\mathcal{S}\text{ for a }3-\text{XOR game}\big\}\text{, }

\displaystyle\ket{\psi_{4\mathrm{XOR}}}\equiv\underset{\text{Players}}{\bigcup}\underset{\mathcal{S}}{\mathrm{sup}}\big\{\text{A player's quantum strategy }\mathcal{S}\text{ for a }4-\text{XOR game}\big\}\text{, }

\displaystyle\ket{\psi_{5\mathrm{XOR}}}\equiv\underset{\text{Players}}{\bigcup}\underset{\mathcal{S}}{\mathrm{sup}}\big\{\text{A player's quantum strategy }\mathcal{S}\text{ for a }5-\text{XOR game}\big\}\text{, }

\displaystyle\ket{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}}}\equiv\underset{\text{Players}}{\bigcup}\underset{\mathcal{S}}{\mathrm{sup}}\big\{\text{A player's quantum strategy }\mathcal{S}\text{ for an }N-\text{XOR game}\big\}\text{, }

respectively, for the
3
-
\mathrm{XOR}
,
4
-
\mathrm{XOR}
,
5
-
\mathrm{XOR}
, and
N
-
\mathrm{XOR}
, games. Given error bounds formulated in previous sections for each
\mathrm{XOR}
game, one obtains error bound inequalities of the form,

\displaystyle\bigg|\bigg|\bigg[\bigg(\bigg(\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}A^{j_{i}}_{i}\bigg)\otimes B_{kl}\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg)-\omega_{3\mathrm{XOR}}\bigg(\pm\mathrm{sign}\big(i_{1},j_{1},\cdots,j_{n}\big)

\displaystyle\times\bigg[\bigg(\bigg(\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}A^{j_{i}}_{k}\bigg)+\bigg(\underset{\text{set }j+1\equiv j\oplus 1}{\underset{i\in\mathcal{Q}_{1},j\in\mathcal{Q}_{2}}{\prod}}A^{j_{i}}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg]\bigg)\bigg]\ket{\psi_{3\mathrm{XOR}}}\bigg|\bigg|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim 3!n^{3}\sqrt{\epsilon}\equiv 1000n^{3}\sqrt{\epsilon}\text{, }

\displaystyle\bigg|\bigg|\bigg[\bigg(\bigg(\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}A^{j_{i}}_{i}\bigg)\otimes B_{kl}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg)-\omega_{4\mathrm{XOR}}\bigg(\pm\mathrm{sign}\big(i_{1},j_{1},\cdots,j_{n}\big)

\displaystyle\times\bigg[\bigg(\bigg(\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}A^{j_{i}}_{k}\bigg)+\bigg(\underset{\text{set }j+1\equiv j\oplus 1}{\underset{i\in\mathcal{Q}_{1},j\in\mathcal{Q}_{2}}{\prod}}A^{j_{i}}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg]\bigg)\bigg]\ket{\psi_{4\mathrm{XOR}}}\bigg|\bigg|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim 4!n^{4}\sqrt{\epsilon}\equiv 100000n^{4}\sqrt{\epsilon}\text{, }

\displaystyle\bigg|\bigg|\bigg[\bigg(\bigg(\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}A^{j_{i}}_{i}\bigg)\otimes B_{kl}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg)-\omega_{5\mathrm{XOR}}\bigg(\pm\mathrm{sign}\big(i_{1},j_{1},\cdots,j_{n}\big)

\displaystyle\times\bigg[\bigg(\bigg(\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}A^{j_{i}}_{k}\bigg)+\bigg(\underset{\text{set }j+1\equiv j\oplus 1}{\underset{i\in\mathcal{Q}_{1},j\in\mathcal{Q}_{2}}{\prod}}A^{j_{i}}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg]\bigg)\bigg]\ket{\psi_{5\mathrm{XOR}}}\bigg|\bigg|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim 5!n^{5}\sqrt{\epsilon}\equiv 1000\sqrt{2}n^{5}\sqrt{\epsilon}\text{, }

\displaystyle\bigg|\bigg|\bigg[\bigg(\bigg(\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}A^{j_{i}}_{i}\bigg)\bigotimes B_{kl}\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq N-2}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)-\omega_{N\mathrm{XOR}}\bigg(\pm\mathrm{sign}\big(i_{1},j_{1},\cdots,j_{n}\big)

\displaystyle\times\bigg[\bigg(\bigg(\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}A^{j_{i}}_{k}\bigg)+\bigg(\underset{\text{set }j+1\equiv j\oplus 1}{\underset{i\in\mathcal{Q}_{1},j\in\mathcal{Q}_{2}}{\prod}}A^{j_{i}}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq N-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg]\bigg)\bigg]\ket{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}}}\bigg|\bigg|^{2}

\lesssim N!n^{N}\sqrt{\epsilon}\times\left\{\!\begin{array}[]{ll@{}>{{}}l}n^{\frac{\#\text{ of players}}{2}+5}\Longleftrightarrow\big(\#\text{ of players}\big)\mathrm{mod}2\equiv 0\\
n^{\lfloor\frac{\#\text{ of players}}{2}\rfloor+5}\Longleftrightarrow\big(\#\text{ of players}\big)\mathrm{mod}2\neq 0\end{array}\right.\text{. }

Proof of Lemma Gen-FFL-Bound
. The desired upper bound for the
3
\mathrm{XOR}
game is obtained first. For the optimal strategy,
\ket{\psi_{3\mathrm{XOR}}}
, it suffices to argue that an upper bound of the desired form holds by making use of the fact that,

\displaystyle\bigg[\bigg[\textbf{I}-\textbf{I}\bigg(\frac{\pm A_{k}+A_{l}}{\sqrt{2}}\bigg)\bigg]\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg]\ket{\psi_{3\mathrm{XOR}}}<\sqrt{3!n\big(n-1\big)\big(n-2\big)}\epsilon\text{, }

which is related to the fact that,

\displaystyle\bigg[\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg]\ket{\psi_{3\mathrm{XOR}}}<\sqrt{n\big(n-1\big)\big(n-2\big)}\epsilon\text{. }

Moreover, from the
\sqrt{2n\big(n-1\big)\big(n-2\big)}\epsilon
upper bound, for the quantum state
\ket{\psi_{3\mathrm{XOR}}}
corresponding to the optimal strategy, the optimal value
\omega_{3\mathrm{XOR}}
, as demonstrated through the statement of the desired inequality to be proved, implies that the operator acting on the 3
\mathrm{XOR}
optimal state,

\displaystyle\bigg[\bigg[\textbf{I}-\omega_{3\mathrm{XOR}}\mathrm{sign}\big(i_{1},j_{1},\cdots,j_{n}\big)\textbf{I}\bigg]\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg]\ket{\psi_{3\mathrm{XOR}}}<n^{3}\sqrt{\epsilon}\text{, }

can be rearranged as,

\displaystyle\bigg[\bigg(\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg)-\omega_{3\mathrm{XOR}}\mathrm{sign}\big(i_{1},j_{1},\cdots,j_{n}\big)\bigg(\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg)\bigg]\ket{\psi_{3\mathrm{XOR}}}\equiv\bigg(\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg)\ket{\psi_{3\mathrm{XOR}}}

\displaystyle-\omega_{3\mathrm{XOR}}\mathrm{sign}\big(i_{1},j_{1},\cdots,j_{n}\big)\bigg(\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg)\ket{\psi_{3\mathrm{XOR}}}\text{, }

has the upper bound,

\displaystyle n^{3}\sqrt{\epsilon}\text{. }

Incorporating the previous estimates, the first of which is from formulating an upper bound for,

\displaystyle\bigg[\bigg[\textbf{I}-\bigg(\frac{\pm A_{k}+A_{l}}{\sqrt{2}}\bigg)\textbf{I}\bigg]\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg]\ket{\psi_{3\mathrm{XOR}}}\text{, }

and the second of which is from formulating an upper bound for,

\displaystyle\bigg(\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg)\ket{\psi_{3\mathrm{XOR}}}-\omega_{3\mathrm{XOR}}\mathrm{sign}\big(i_{1},j_{1},\cdots,j_{n}\big)\bigg(\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg)\ket{\psi_{3\mathrm{XOR}}}\text{, }

demonstrates that the desired upper bound takes the desired form, from the observation that,

\displaystyle\bigg|\bigg|\bigg[\bigg(\bigg(\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}A^{j_{i}}_{i}\bigg)\otimes B_{kl}\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg)-\omega_{3\mathrm{XOR}}\bigg(\pm\mathrm{sign}\big(i_{1},j_{1},\cdots,j_{n}\big)\bigg[\bigg(\bigg(\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}A^{j_{i}}_{k}\bigg)

\displaystyle+\bigg(\underset{\text{set }j+1\equiv j\oplus 1}{\underset{i\in\mathcal{Q}_{1},j\in\mathcal{Q}_{2}}{\prod}}A^{j_{i}}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg]\bigg)\bigg]\ket{\psi_{3\mathrm{XOR}}}\bigg|\bigg|^{2}\text{, }

implying,

\displaystyle\sqrt{3!n\big(n-1\big)\big(n-2\big)}\epsilon+\big(100\omega_{3\mathrm{XOR}}\big)n^{3}\sqrt{\epsilon}<\sqrt{3!n\big(n-1\big)\big(n-2\big)}\epsilon+\big(1000\big)n^{3}\sqrt{\epsilon}

\displaystyle<\sqrt{\big(3!n\big(n-1\big)\big(n-2\big)+10000n^{7}\big)\epsilon}

\displaystyle\equiv\sqrt{3!\big(n^{3}-3n^{2}+2n+10000n^{7}\big)\epsilon}

\displaystyle\equiv\sqrt{3!\big(\big(n^{3}\big(1+10000n^{4}\big)-n\big(3n+2\big)\big)\epsilon}

\displaystyle<\sqrt{3!\big(n^{3}\big(10001n^{4}\big)-n\big(3n+2\big)\big)}\big(10\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)

\displaystyle<\sqrt{3!\big(n^{3}\big(10001n^{4}\big)-n\big(10n\big)\big)}\big(10\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)

\displaystyle\equiv\sqrt{3!n^{2}\big(n\big(10001n^{4}-10\big)\big)}\big(10\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)<\sqrt{3!n^{2}\big(n\big(10002n^{4}\big)}\big(10\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)

\displaystyle\equiv\sqrt{10002n^{7}}\big(10\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)<\sqrt{10002n^{8}}\big(10\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)<10\sqrt{10002}n^{3}\sqrt{\epsilon}\approx 1000n^{3}\sqrt{\epsilon}\text{. }

As stated in the result at the beginning of the subsection, the final upper bound obtained for the
3
-
\mathrm{XOR}
game satisfies,

\displaystyle\sqrt{3!n\big(n-1\big)\big(n-2\big)}\epsilon+\big(100\omega_{3\mathrm{XOR}}\big)n^{3}\sqrt{\epsilon}\lesssim 3!n^{3}\sqrt{\epsilon}\equiv 1000n^{3}\sqrt{\epsilon}\text{. }

For the 4-player XOR game, similar computations under the square root imply that an upper bound for the action of mapping
i+1\equiv i\oplus 1
, or
j+1\equiv j\oplus 1
, or
i+1\equiv i\oplus 1
, and
j+1\equiv j\oplus 1
, simultaneously, can be deduced from the following assumptions on the optimal strategy,
\ket{\psi_{4\mathrm{XOR}}}
. That is, to upper bound,

\displaystyle\bigg|\bigg|\bigg[\bigg(\bigg(\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}A^{j_{i}}_{i}\bigg)\otimes B_{kl}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg)-\omega_{4\mathrm{XOR}}\bigg(\pm\mathrm{sign}\big(i_{1},j_{1},\cdots,j_{n}\big)\bigg[\bigg(\bigg(\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}A^{j_{i}}_{k}\bigg)

\displaystyle+\bigg(\underset{\text{set }j+1\equiv j\oplus 1}{\underset{i\in\mathcal{Q}_{1},j\in\mathcal{Q}_{2}}{\prod}}A^{j_{i}}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg]\bigg)\bigg]\ket{\psi_{4\mathrm{XOR}}}\bigg|\bigg|^{2}\text{, }

fix
\epsilon^{\prime}\neq\epsilon
sufficiently small, from which the computations for upper bounds the Frobenius norm result from the inequalities,

\displaystyle\bigg[\bigg[\textbf{I}-\textbf{I}\bigg(\frac{\pm A_{k}+A_{l}}{\sqrt{2}}\bigg)\bigg]\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg]\ket{\psi_{4\mathrm{XOR}}}<\sqrt{4!n\big(n-1\big)\big(n-2\big)\big(n-3\big)}\epsilon^{\prime}\text{, }

\displaystyle\bigg[\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg]\ket{\psi_{4\mathrm{XOR}}}<\sqrt{n\big(n-1\big)\big(n-2\big)}\epsilon^{\prime}\text{, }

\displaystyle\bigg[\bigg[\textbf{I}-\omega_{4\mathrm{XOR}}\mathrm{sign}\big(i_{1},j_{1},\cdots,j_{n}\big)\textbf{I}\bigg]\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg]\ket{\psi_{4\mathrm{XOR}}}<n^{4}\sqrt{\epsilon^{\prime}}\text{. }

To lighten the notation in the computations below, denote,

\displaystyle\epsilon\equiv\epsilon^{\prime}\text{. }

Hence, the desired upper bound dependent upon the number of players in the game takes the form,

\displaystyle\sqrt{4!n\big(n-1\big)\big(n-2\big)\big(n-3\big)}\epsilon+100\omega_{4\mathrm{XOR}}n^{4}\sqrt{\epsilon}<\sqrt{4!n\big(n-1\big)\big(n-2\big)\big(n-3\big)}\epsilon

\displaystyle+1000w_{4\mathrm{XOR}}\sqrt{n^{8}\epsilon}<\sqrt{\big(4!n\big(n-1\big)\big(n-2\big)\big(n-3\big)\big)\epsilon+10000\omega_{4\mathrm{XOR}}\big(n^{8}\epsilon\big)}

\displaystyle\equiv\sqrt{\big(4!n\big(n-1\big)\big(n-2\big)\big(n-3\big)+10000\omega_{4\mathrm{XOR}}n^{8}\big)\epsilon}

\displaystyle\equiv\sqrt{\big(20n^{4}-10n^{3}+12n^{2}-4n^{3}+10n^{2}-12n+1000\omega_{4\mathrm{XOR}}n^{8}\big)\epsilon}

\displaystyle<100\sqrt{2n^{4}-10n^{3}+12n^{2}-4n^{3}+15n^{2}+10000\omega_{4\mathrm{XOR}}n^{8}}\sqrt{\epsilon}

\displaystyle<1000\sqrt{\big(2n^{4}-10n^{3}+12n^{2}-40n^{3}+10000\omega_{4\mathrm{XOR}}n^{8}\big)\epsilon}

\displaystyle<10000\sqrt{\big(2n^{4}-10n^{3}+12n^{2}+100n^{2}+10000\omega_{4\mathrm{XOR}}n^{8}\big)\epsilon}

\displaystyle<10000\sqrt{\big(2n^{4}-10n^{3}+113n^{2}+10000\omega_{4\mathrm{XOR}}n^{8}\big)\epsilon}

\displaystyle<10000\sqrt{\big(2n^{4}-10n^{3}+113n^{3}+10000\omega_{4\mathrm{XOR}}n^{8}\big)\epsilon}

\displaystyle<10000\sqrt{\big(2n^{4}+100n^{3}+10000\omega_{4\mathrm{XOR}}n^{8}\big)\epsilon}

\displaystyle<10000\sqrt{\big(105n^{4}+10000\omega_{4\mathrm{XOR}}n^{8}\big)\epsilon}

\displaystyle<10000\sqrt{\big(10500n^{8}+10000\omega_{4\mathrm{XOR}}n^{8}\big)\epsilon}

\displaystyle<10000\sqrt{10500n^{8}\epsilon}

\displaystyle<100000n^{4}\sqrt{\epsilon}\text{. }

As stated in the result at the beginning of the subsection, the final upper bound obtained for the
4
-
\mathrm{XOR}
game satisfies,

\displaystyle\sqrt{4!n\big(n-1\big)\big(n-2\big)\big(n-3\big)}\epsilon+100\omega_{4\mathrm{XOR}}n^{4}\sqrt{\epsilon}\lesssim 4!n^{4}\sqrt{\epsilon}\equiv 100000n^{4}\sqrt{\epsilon}\text{. }

To upper bound,

\displaystyle\bigg|\bigg|\bigg[\bigg(\bigg(\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}A^{j_{i}}_{i}\bigg)\otimes B_{kl}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg)-\omega_{5\mathrm{XOR}}\bigg(\pm\mathrm{sign}\big(i_{1},j_{1},\cdots,j_{n}\big)\bigg[\bigg(\bigg(\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}A^{j_{i}}_{k}\bigg)

\displaystyle+\bigg(\underset{\text{set }j+1\equiv j\oplus 1}{\underset{i\in\mathcal{Q}_{1},j\in\mathcal{Q}_{2}}{\prod}}A^{j_{i}}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg]\bigg)\bigg]\ket{\psi_{5\mathrm{XOR}}}\bigg|\bigg|^{2}\text{, }

fix
\epsilon^{\prime\prime}\neq\epsilon^{\prime}\neq\epsilon
sufficiently small, from which the assumptions for the 5-
\mathrm{XOR}
game include,

\displaystyle\bigg[\bigg[\textbf{I}-\textbf{I}\bigg(\frac{\pm A_{k}+A_{l}}{\sqrt{2}}\bigg)\bigg]\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg]\ket{\psi_{5\mathrm{XOR}}}

\displaystyle<\sqrt{5!n\big(n-1\big)\big(n-2\big)\big(n-3\big)\big(n-4\big)}\epsilon^{\prime\prime}\text{, }

\displaystyle\bigg[\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg]\ket{\psi_{5\mathrm{XOR}}}<\sqrt{n\big(n-1\big)\big(n-2\big)}\epsilon^{\prime\prime}\text{, }

\displaystyle\bigg[\bigg[\textbf{I}-\omega_{4\mathrm{XOR}}\mathrm{sign}\big(i_{1},j_{1},\cdots,j_{n}\big)\textbf{I}\bigg]\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg]\ket{\psi_{5\mathrm{XOR}}}<n^{5}\sqrt{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}\text{. }

To lighten the notation in the computations below, denote,

\displaystyle\epsilon\equiv\epsilon^{\prime\prime}\text{. }

Hence, the desired upper bound dependent upon the number of players in the game takes the form,

\displaystyle\sqrt{5!n\big(n-1\big)\big(n-2\big)\big(n-3\big)\big(n-4\big)}+100\omega_{5\mathrm{XOR}}n^{5}\sqrt{\epsilon}

\displaystyle<\sqrt{5!n\big(n-1\big)\big(n-2\big)\big(n-3\big)\big(n-4\big)\epsilon+1000\omega_{5\mathrm{XOR}}n^{10}\epsilon}

\displaystyle\equiv\sqrt{\big(5!n\big(n-1\big)\big(n-2\big)\big(n-3\big)\big(n-4\big)+1000\omega_{5\mathrm{XOR}}n^{10}\big)\epsilon}

\displaystyle<\sqrt{\big(5!n\big(n-1\big)\big(n-2\big)\big(n-3\big)\big(n-4\big)+10000n^{10}\big)\epsilon}

\displaystyle<\sqrt{\big(20n^{5}-8n^{4}+120n^{4}-40n^{3}+12n^{2}-48n\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{-2n^{4}+8n^{3}+10n^{3}-40n^{2}+10000n^{10}\big)\epsilon}

\displaystyle\equiv\sqrt{\big(20n^{5}+112n^{4}-22n^{3}-28n^{2}-48n+10000n^{10}\big)\epsilon}

\displaystyle<\sqrt{\big(20n^{5}+112n^{4}-22n^{3}-40n^{2}+10000n^{10}\big)\epsilon}

\displaystyle<\sqrt{\big(20n^{5}+112n^{4}-10n^{3}+10000n^{10}\big)\epsilon}

\displaystyle<\sqrt{\big(20n^{5}+120n^{4}+10000n^{10}\big)\epsilon}<\sqrt{\big(100n^{10}+10000n^{10}\big)\epsilon}

\displaystyle<\sqrt{20000n^{10}\epsilon}<\sqrt{20000}\big(n^{5}\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)<1000\sqrt{2}\big(n^{5}\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)\text{. }

As stated in the result at the beginning of the subsection, the final upper bound obtained for the
5
-
\mathrm{XOR}
game satisfies,

\displaystyle\sqrt{5!n\big(n-1\big)\big(n-2\big)\big(n-3\big)\big(n-4\big)}+100\omega_{5\mathrm{XOR}}n^{5}\sqrt{\epsilon}\lesssim 5!n^{5}\sqrt{\epsilon}\equiv 1000\sqrt{2}\big(n^{5}\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)\text{. }

In comparison to the upper bounds that have been previously obtained for a few players in the
\mathrm{XOR}
setting, for an arbitrary number of players the following upper bound is dependent, up to leading order, upon,

\displaystyle 2n^{\#\text{ of players}}\text{, }

with other leading orders being determined by,

\displaystyle\mathrm{O}\big(n^{\#\text{ of players}}-1\big),\cdots,\mathrm{O}\big(n^{\#\text{ of players}}-\big(n-1\big)\big),\cdots,\mathrm{O}\big(1\big)\text{. }

To upper bound,

\displaystyle\bigg|\bigg|\bigg[\bigg(\bigg(\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}A^{j_{i}}_{i}\bigg)\bigotimes B_{kl}\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq N-2}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)-\omega_{N\mathrm{XOR}}\bigg(\pm\mathrm{sign}\big(i_{1},j_{1},\cdots,j_{n}\big)

\displaystyle\times\bigg[\bigg(\bigg(\underset{1\leq i\leq n}{\prod}A^{j_{i}}_{k}\bigg)+\bigg(\underset{\text{set }j+1\equiv j\oplus 1}{\underset{i\in\mathcal{Q}_{1},j\in\mathcal{Q}_{2}}{\prod}}A^{j_{i}}_{k}\bigg)\bigg)\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq k\leq N-1}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{k}\bigg)\bigg]\bigg)\bigg]\ket{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}}}\bigg|\bigg|^{2}\text{, }

fix
\epsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}\neq\epsilon^{\prime\prime}\neq\epsilon^{\prime}\neq\epsilon
sufficiently small, from which the assumptions for the N
\mathrm{XOR}
game include,

\displaystyle\bigg[\bigg[\textbf{I}-\textbf{I}\bigg(\frac{\pm A_{k}+A_{l}}{\sqrt{2}}\bigg)\bigg]\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq z\leq N-5}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{z}\bigg)\bigg]\ket{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}}}\text{, }

\displaystyle\bigg(\underset{1\leq z\leq N-5}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{z}\bigg)\ket{\psi_{5\mathrm{XOR}}}\text{, }

\displaystyle\bigg[\bigg[\textbf{I}-\omega_{4\mathrm{XOR}}\mathrm{sign}\big(i_{1},j_{1},\cdots,j_{n}\big)\textbf{I}\bigg]\bigotimes\bigg(\underset{1\leq z\leq N-5}{\bigotimes}\textbf{I}_{z}\bigg)\bigg]\ket{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}}}\text{, }

which can be upper bounded as follows, with,

\displaystyle\bigg[\bigg[\textbf{I}-\textbf{I}\bigg(\frac{\pm A_{k}+A_{l}}{\sqrt{2}}\bigg)\bigg]\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\overset{N-5}{\cdots}\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg]\ket{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}}}

\displaystyle<\sqrt{N!n\big(n-1\big)\big(n-2\big)\big(n-3\big)\big(n-4\big)}\epsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}\text{, }

\displaystyle\bigg[\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\overset{N-5}{\cdots}\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg]\ket{\psi_{5\mathrm{XOR}}}<\sqrt{n\big(n-1\big)\big(n-2\big)}\epsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}\text{, }

\displaystyle\bigg[\bigg[\textbf{I}-\omega_{4\mathrm{XOR}}\mathrm{sign}\big(i_{1},j_{1},\cdots,j_{n}\big)\textbf{I}\bigg]\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\textbf{I}\otimes\overset{N-5}{\cdots}\otimes\textbf{I}\bigg]\ket{\psi_{N\mathrm{XOR}}}

\displaystyle<n^{N}\sqrt{\epsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}}\text{, }

respectively. To lighten the notation in the computations below, denote,

\displaystyle\epsilon\equiv\epsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}\text{. }

Hence, the desired upper bound dependent upon the number of players in the game takes the form,

\displaystyle\sqrt{N!n\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq\#\text{ of players}-1}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)\epsilon+100\omega_{N\mathrm{XOR}}n^{\#\text{ of players}}\epsilon}

\displaystyle<\sqrt{N!n\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq\#\text{ of players}-1}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)\epsilon+1000n^{\#\text{ of players}}\epsilon}\text{, }

which can be further rearranged as,

\displaystyle\sqrt{\bigg(N!n\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq\#\text{ of players}-1}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)+1000n^{\#\text{ of players}}\bigg)\epsilon}

\displaystyle<\sqrt{\bigg(N!n\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq\#\text{ of players}-1}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)+1000n^{\#\text{ of players}}\bigg)}

\displaystyle\times\big(10\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)

\displaystyle<\sqrt{2N!n\big(n-\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)\big)\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq\#\text{ of players}-2}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)+1000n^{\#\text{ of players}}}

\displaystyle\times\big(10\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)

\displaystyle\equiv\sqrt{\big(2N!n^{2}-n\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)\big)\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq\#\text{ of players}-2}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)+1000n^{\#\text{ of players}}}

\displaystyle\times\big(10\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)

\displaystyle<\sqrt{20N!n^{2}\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq\#\text{ of players}-2}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)-n\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq\#\text{ of players}-2}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{+1000n^{\#\text{ of players}}}\big(10\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)

\displaystyle<\sqrt{20N!n^{2}\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq\#\text{ of players}-2}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)-n\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)\big(n-\big(\#\text{ of players}-2\big)\big)\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{\times\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq\#\text{ of players}-3}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)+1000n^{\#\text{ of players}}}\big(11\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)\text{. }

As the product over the number of players is taken, the final term above can be upper bounded with,

\displaystyle\sqrt{20N!n^{2}\big(n-\big(\#\text{ of players}-2\big)\big)\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq\#\text{ of players}-3}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)-\bigg(n^{2}\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{+\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)\big(\#\text{ of players}-2\big)\bigg)\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq\#\text{ of players}-3}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)+1000n^{\#\text{ of players}}}\big(11\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)

\displaystyle<\sqrt{20N!n^{2}\big(n\big(\#\text{ of players}-2\big)\big)\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq\#\text{ of players}-3}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)-\frac{1}{2}\bigg(n^{2}\big(\#\text{ of players}\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{-1\big)+\big(\#\text{of players}-1\big)\big(\#\text{of players}-2\big)\bigg)\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq\#\text{of players}-3}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)+1000n^{\#\text{ of players}}}\big(11\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)

\displaystyle\equiv\sqrt{20N!n^{2}\big(n-\big(\#\text{of players}-2\big)\big)\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq\#\text{of players}-3}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)-\frac{1}{2}\bigg(n^{2}\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{+\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)\big(\#\text{ of players}-2\big)\bigg)\big(n-\big(\#\text{ of players}-3\big)\big)\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq\#\text{ of players}-4}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{+1000n^{\#\text{ of players}}}\big(11\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)\text{. }

(*)

Proceeding, the expression above, denoted with
(*)
, equals,

\displaystyle(\textit{*})\equiv\sqrt{20N!n^{2}\big(n-\big(\#\text{ of players}-2\big)\big)\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq\#\text{ of players}-3}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)-\frac{1}{2}\bigg(-n^{2}\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{\times\big(\#\text{ of players}-3\big)\bigg)+n^{3}\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)+n\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)\big(\#\text{ of players }-2\big)\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{-\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)\big(\#\text{ of players }-2\big)\big(\#\text{ of players}-3\big)\bigg)\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq\#\text{ of players}-5}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{+1000n^{\#\text{ of players }}}\big(11\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)

\displaystyle<\sqrt{20N!n^{2}\big(n-\big(\#\text{ of players}-2\big)\big)\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq\#\text{ of players}-3}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)-\frac{1}{2}\bigg(n^{2}\big(\#\text{ of players}\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{-1\big)\big(\#\text{ of players}-3\big)+n^{3}\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)+n\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)\big(\#\text{ of players}-2\big)\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{-\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)\big(\#\text{ of players}-2\big)\big(\#\text{ of players}-3\big)\bigg)\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq\#\text{ of players}}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{+1000n^{\#\text{ of players}}}\big(11\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)

\displaystyle<\sqrt{20N!n^{2}\big(n-\big(\#\text{ of players}-2\big)\big)\big(n-\#\text{ of players}-3\big)\bigg)\big(n-1\big)-\frac{1}{2}\bigg(-n^{2}\big(\#\text{of players }-1\big)\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{\times\big(\#\text{ of players}-3\big)\big(n-\big(\#\text{of players}-4\big)\big)+n^{3}\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)\big(n-\big(\#\text{ of players}-4\big)\big)\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{+n\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)\big(\#\text{ of players}-2\big)\big(n-\big(\#\text{ of players}-4\big)\big)\bigg)\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq\#\text{ of players}-5}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{+1000n^{\#\text{ of players}}}\big(11\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)

\displaystyle<\sqrt{20N!n^{2}\big(n-\big(\#\text{ of players}-2\big)\big)\big(n-\big(\#\text{ of players}-3\big)\big)\times\cdots\times\big(n-1\big)-\frac{1}{4}\big(-n^{2}\big(\#\text{ of players}\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{-1\big)\big(\#\text{ of players}-3\big)\big(\#\text{ of players}-4\big)+n^{3}\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)\big(\#\text{ of players}-4\big)\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{+n\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)\big(\#\text{ of players}-2\big)\big(\#\text{ of players}-4\big)\big)\bigg)\bigg(n^{\#\text{ of players - 5}}\bigg)\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{+1000n^{\#\text{ of players}}}\big(11\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)\text{. }

(**)

Proceeding,

\displaystyle(\textit{**})<\sqrt{20N!n^{2}\bigg(n-\big(\#\text{ of players}-2\big)\big)\big(n-\big(\#\text{ of players}-3\big)\big)\times\cdots\times\big(n-1\big)-\frac{1}{4}\big(-n^{2}\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{\times\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)\big(\#\text{ of players}-3\big)\big(\#\text{ of players}-4\big)+n^{3}\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{\times\big(\#\text{ of players}-4\big)+n\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)\big(\#\text{ of players}-2\big)\big(\#\text{ of players}-4\big)\bigg)\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{\times\bigg(n^{\#\text{ of players}-5}\bigg)+1000n^{\#\text{ of players}}}\big(11\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)

\displaystyle\equiv\sqrt{20N!n^{2}\bigg(n-\big(\#\text{ of players}-2\big)\big(n-\big(\#\text{ of players}-3\big)\big)\times\cdots\times\big(n-1\big)-\frac{1}{4}\big(-n^{2}\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{\times\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)\big(\#\text{ of players}-3\big)\big(\#\text{ of players}-4\big)+n\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{\times\big(\#\text{ of players}-2\big)\big(\#\text{ of players}-4\big)\bigg)\bigg(n^{\#\text{ of players}-5}\bigg)+1000n^{\#\text{ of players}}}\big(11\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)

\displaystyle\equiv\sqrt{\bigg(20N!n^{3}-100n^{2}\big(\#\text{ of players}-2\big)\bigg(\big(n-\big(\#\text{ of players}-3\big)\big)\times\cdots\times\big(n-1\big)\bigg)\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{-\frac{1}{4}\bigg(-n^{2}\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)\big(\#\text{ of players}-3\big)\big(\#\text{ of players}-4\big)+n^{3}\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{\times\big(\#\text{ of players}-4\big)n^{\#\text{of players}-5}+n\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)\big(\#\text{ of players}-2\big)\bigg)\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{\times\big(\#\text{ of players}-4\big)n^{\#\text{ of players}-5}\bigg)+1000n^{\#\text{ of players}}}\big(11\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)\text{. }

(***)

The final expression above can be further rearranged to obtain the desired upper bound, from the observations that,

\displaystyle(\textit{***})<\sqrt{\bigg(20N!n^{3}-50n^{2}\big(n-2\big)\bigg)\big(n-1\big)^{\#\text{ of players}-5}-\frac{1}{4}\bigg(-n^{2}\big(\#\text{ of players - 1}\big)^{3}n^{\#\text{ of players}-5}\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{+n^{3}\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)^{2}n^{\#\text{ of players}-5}+n\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)^{3}n^{\#\text{ of players}-5}\bigg)+1000n^{\#\text{ of players}}}

\displaystyle\times\big(11\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)

\displaystyle\equiv\sqrt{\bigg(20N!n^{3}-50n^{2}\big(n-2\big)\big)\big(n-1\big)^{\#\text{ of players}-3}\bigg)-\frac{1}{4}\big(\big(\#\text{ of players}\big)-1\big)^{2}n\bigg(-n\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{\times n^{\#\text{ of players}-5}+n^{\big(\#\text{ of players}-5\big)+1}+\big(\#\text{ of players}n^{\#\text{ of players}-5}\bigg)+1000n^{\#\text{ of players}}}\big(11\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)

\displaystyle<\sqrt{\bigg(20N!n^{\#\text{ of players}}-50n^{\#\text{ of players}}-\bigg)-\frac{1}{4}n^{3}\bigg(-n\big(\#\text{ of players}-1\big)n^{\#\text{ of players}-5}\cdots}

\displaystyle\sqrt{+n^{\#\text{ of players}-5}+\big(\#\text{ of players }-5\big)n^{\#\text{ of players}-5}\bigg)+1000n^{\#\text{of players}-5}}\big(11\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)

\displaystyle<\sqrt{n^{\#\text{ of players}}\bigg(n^{\#\text{ of players}}-n^{3}\bigg(n^{\#\text{ of players}-4}+2n^{\#\text{ of players}-5}\bigg)\bigg)+1000n^{\#\text{of players}-5}}\big(110\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)

\displaystyle<\sqrt{\bigg(n^{\#\text{ of players}}-n^{3}\bigg(-n^{\#\text{ of players}-5}+2n^{\#\text{ of players}-5}\bigg)+1000n^{\#\text{ of players}}\bigg)}\big(120\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)\text{.}

(****)

The final desired upper bound,

\mathcal{I}\equiv\big(130N!n^{N}\big)\sqrt{\epsilon}\times\left\{\!\begin{array}[]{ll@{}>{{}}l}n^{\frac{\#\text{ of players}}{2}+5}\Longleftrightarrow\big(\#\text{ of players}\big)\mathrm{mod}2\equiv 0\\
n^{\lfloor\frac{\#\text{ of players}}{2}\rfloor+5}\Longleftrightarrow\big(\#\text{ of players}\big)\mathrm{mod}2\neq 0\end{array}\right.\text{, }

for the N
\mathrm{XOR}
game above is obtained from the observations that,

\displaystyle(\textit{****})<\sqrt{N!n^{\#\text{ of players}-3}\bigg(1+n^{6}-2n^{5}+1000\bigg)}\big(120\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)<\sqrt{N!n^{\#\text{ of players}-3}\bigg(1+n^{6}+998n^{5}\bigg)}

\displaystyle\times\big(120\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)

\displaystyle<\sqrt{N!n^{\#\text{ of players}-3}\bigg(1+999n^{6}\bigg)}\big(120\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)<\sqrt{N!n^{\#\text{ of players}}n^{8}}\big(120\sqrt{\epsilon}\big)<\mathcal{I}\text{.}

As stated in the result at the beginning of the subsection, the final upper bound obtained for the
N
-
\mathrm{XOR}
game satisfies,

\displaystyle\sqrt{N!n\bigg(\underset{1\leq j\leq\#\text{ of players}-1}{\prod}\big(n-j\big)\bigg)\epsilon+100\omega_{N\mathrm{XOR}}n^{\#\text{ of players}}\epsilon}\lesssim N!n^{N}\sqrt{\epsilon}\text{. }

With all of the desired upper bounds obtained, we conclude the argument.

3
Conclusion

3.1
Overview

In the final section, we provide a recapitulation of the error bounds obtained in the previous subsections, in addition to several comparisons between two-player, and higher-player, settings. To further develop, and elaborate, upon the rigidity, and structure, of error bounds for XOR,
\mathrm{XOR}^{*}
, and FFL games, we appealed to several aspects of Quantum information theory, ranging from: nonlocality, and contextuality, of the observables that each player prepares when interacting with the referee; paradoxical, and unexpected, aspects of Quantum information arising from error bounds which capture how the optimal value of the game can change; connections with Representation theory through intertwining operations which can transform tensor product representations of player observables into other representations; and several other themes. At the interface of Classical, and Quantum information processing, further examining sources of Quantum advantage that players can assume when making use of the nonlocality of information not only is of interest to further explore under less rigid assumptions on error bounds, but also for the prospects of determining computational tasks that can be executed on near term hardware. Despite the fact that the arguments developed in this work were developed independently of experiments, determining the expected runtime for obtaining exact expressions, or approximations, to primal feasible solutions for 3-XOR, 4-XOR, 5-XOR, N-XOR, FFL, and strong parallel repetition of XOR and FFL games, remains of interest. Besides such possible expansions, and elaborations, on the approach provided in this work, the two-player framework was expanded upon by: (1) providing expressions for game matrices, semidefinite programs, and associated duality gaps for multiplayer XOR games, and the FFL game; (2) upper bounding the multiplayer Frobenius norm; (3) demonstrating that the Frobenius norm has unit norm; (4) generalizing Schur’s lemma to a multidimensional setting corresponding to an arbitrary number of players; (5) obtaining several upper bound estimates for the previous items for XOR and FFL games under strong parallel repetition; (6) proposing several avenues for expanding arguments for other classes of multiplayer games.

3.2
\mathrm{XOR}
and
\mathrm{FFL}
games: generalizations of error bounds

To quantitatively determine how the optimal value, whether classical or quantum, for a game can change depending upon the observables of each player, we list several results previously obtained in this work which could be further studied to generalize error bounds for other game-theoretic settings of interest. First, recall, as a generalization of the two-player error bound, [37],

\displaystyle\underset{1\leq i<j\leq n}{\sum}\bigg[\text{ }\bigg|\bigg|\bigg[\big(\frac{A_{i}+A_{j}}{\sqrt{2}}\big)\otimes I\bigg]\ket{\psi}-\big[I\otimes B_{ij}\big]\ket{\psi}\bigg|\bigg|^{2}+\bigg|\bigg|\bigg[\big(\frac{A_{i}-A_{j}}{\sqrt{2}}\big)\otimes I\bigg]\ket{\psi}

\displaystyle-\big[I\otimes B_{ji}\big]\ket{\psi}\bigg|\bigg|^{2}\text{ }\bigg]\leq 2n\big(n-1\big)\epsilon\text{, }
